.
Graphs from Example
1.58

Example 1.58
.

We give two examples to help the reader understand positive graphs and exchangeable graphs. Consider the LG model
(x_{1}^{4}+x_{2}^{5},\mu_{4}\times\mu_{5})
. We consider the smooth connected
W
-spin graph
\Gamma
depicted in Figure
7
(A) with three internal tails
t_{z_{1}},t_{z_{2}},t_{z_{3}}
and three boundary tails
t_{x_{1}},t_{x_{2}},t_{x_{3}}
adjacent to an open vertex
v
. We will assume that
t_{x_{1}}
is singly twisted with respect to
1
,
t_{x_{2}}
is singly twisted with respect to
2
, and
t_{x_{3}}
is a root (i.e., fully twisted). We suppose that the twist vectors for
t_{z_{1}},t_{z_{2}},
and
t_{z_{3}}
are
(1,1)
,
(2,2)
, and
(2,3)
, respectively. One can check that
\Gamma
is a graded
W
-spin graph as in Definition
1.41
(2).

One can then see in Figure
7
(B) an example of an exchangeable graph
\Lambda_{1}\in\partial^{\textup{exchange}}\Gamma
. Lastly, one can check in Figure
7
(C) that
\Lambda_{2}\in\partial^{+}\Gamma
by computing
(\text{tw}(q_{i}),{\text{alt}}(q_{i}))
at the two half-nodes, as required in Definition
1.40
to ensure that
\Lambda_{2}
is a genus zero
W
-spin dual graph with a lifting. Indeed, the half-node
q_{1}
adjacent to
v_{1}
has
\text{tw}(q_{1})=(0,2)
and
{\text{alt}}(q_{1})=(1,0)
and
q_{2}
adjacent to
v_{2}
has
\text{tw}(q_{2})=(2,1)
and
{\text{alt}}(q_{2})=(0,1)
. Thus, from Definition
1.53
, we see that
q_{1}
is positive for the
2^{nd}
grading and
q_{2}
is positive for the
1^{st}
grading.

Lastly, we have in Figure
7
(D) an example of a relevant graph
\Lambda_{3}\in\partial^{0}\Gamma
. One can check, in similar notation above that the half-node
q_{1}
adjacent to
v_{1}
has
\text{tw}(q_{1})=(0,3)
and
{\text{alt}}(q_{1})=(0,1)
and
q_{2}
adjacent to
v_{2}
has
\text{tw}(q_{2})=(2,0)
and
{\text{alt}}(q_{2})=(1,0)
.

2.
Moduli, bundles and orientation

2.1.
The moduli

This subsection follows §3.2 in
[
5
]
.

Fan, Jarvis, and Ruan constructed a moduli space
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}^{\text{FJR},W}
consisting of compact stable
W
-spin orbicurves, for which they then provide an enumerative
theory. This moduli space does not precisely coincide with our moduli space
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}^{W}
of closed
W
-spin curves. This is due to the treatment of anchors and gradings
which rigidify the curves, as well as choice of stabilizer group at orbifold points (see below). The following theorem is essentially carrying out the proof of Theorem 2.2.6 of
[
15
]
in our context of

W=\sum_{i=1}^{n}x_{i}^{r_{i}}
with the maximal symmetry group, with some
slight differences which arise, for the most part, because of different
automorphism groups.

Theorem 2.1
.

\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}^{W}
is a smooth Deligne-Mumford stack with projective coarse moduli. Moreover, if we let
\operatorname{st}:\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}^{W}\rightarrow\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}
be the morphism given by forgetting the spin bundles and orbifold structure, then
\operatorname{st}
is a flat, proper, and quasi-finite (but not representable) morphism.

We point out the differences from our moduli space and the one outlined in
[
15
]
. First, with our definitions, stabilizer groups of special points are always
\mu_{d}
, while Fan, Jarvis and Ruan choose a stabilizer group
\mu_{e}
at a special point
x

so that the representation of
\mu_{e}
on the direct sum of the fibres of the spin bundles at
x
is faithful. This changes the universal curve over
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}^{W}
. In addition, it changes automorphism groups of nodal curves which lie at the boundary of the moduli space (see Observation
1.15
), so that our moduli space is a root stack over the moduli space of
[
15
]
.

However, there is a further distinction arising from twisted spin structures. Our universal twisted spin bundles are twists of the universal spin bundles of
[
15
]
. Further, there may be an additional change in automorphism group arising due to the notion of grading (Definition
1.7
) at anchors with twist
r-1
, as the data of a grading partially rigidifies the curve. In this case, our moduli space is an unramified
r
-fold cover in the orbifold sense (but does not change the coarse moduli spaces) of the moduli space of
[
15
]
, at least away from the boundary.

By stratifying the moduli space
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}^{W}
by twists in the spin structure, it decomposes into open and closed substacks,

(2.1)

\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}^{W}=\bigsqcup_{((b_{11},\ldots,b_{1a}),\ldots,(b_{n1},\ldots,b_{na}))}\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,((b_{11},\ldots,b_{1a})\ldots,(b_{n1},\ldots,b_{na}))}^{W},

where
b_{ij}\in\{-1,0,\ldots,r_{j}-1\}
for each
i,j
and
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,((b_{11},\ldots,b_{1a})\ldots,(b_{n1},\ldots,b_{na}))}^{W}
denotes the substack of graded closed
genus
g

W
-spin curves with
W
-spin structures having twist
(b_{i1},\ldots,b_{ia})
at the
i
th marked point for all
i
. For any choice of

\{(b_{i1},\ldots,b_{ia})\}_{i\in[n]}
such that Equation (
1.14
) holds for all
j\in[a]
, we know that the moduli space of compact stable genus 0
W
-spin orbicurves

\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,((b_{11},\ldots,b_{1a})\ldots,(b_{n1},\ldots,b_{na}))}^{W}
with the corresponding prescribed twists is non-empty, by the results mentioned in Proposition
1.26
. Moreover, in this case, the moduli space has coarse moduli isomorphic to
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,n}
and generic additional isotropy
\prod_{i\in[a]}\mathbb{Z}/r_{i}\mathbb{Z}
. The isomorphism of coarse moduli is given by the smooth map
\text{For}_{\text{\text{spin}}}
that forgets the spin structures.

To generalize the construction of the moduli space to the open setting, we introduce some new notation.

Definition 2.2
.

For a genus zero, anchored, pre-stable, smooth dual graph
\Gamma
, we denote by
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{\Gamma}
the set of isomorphism classes of smooth marked disks whose dual graph
\Gamma^{\prime}
after smoothing gives
\Gamma
. We denote by
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{\Gamma}^{W}
the set of all isomorphism classes of pre-graded
W
-spin disks whose corresponding dual graph
\Gamma^{\prime}
satisfies
{\text{sm}}(\text{for}_{\text{spin}}(\Gamma^{\prime}))=\Gamma
. For a smooth pre-graded
W
-spin dual graph
\Gamma
we denote by
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{\Gamma}^{W}
the set of all isomorphism classes of graded
W
-spin disks whose corresponding graph, after smoothing, is
\Gamma
.

For some common cases, we introduce special notation. Recall Notation
1.45
.
We write

{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{0,B,I}={\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{\Gamma_{0,B,I}},\quad{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}^{W}_{0,B,I}={\overline{\mathcal{M}}}^{W}_{\Gamma_{0,B,I}},\quad{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{0,B,I}^{\text{labeled}}={\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{\Gamma_{0,B,I}^{\text{labeled}}},\quad{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{0,B,I}^{W,\text{labeled}}={\overline{\mathcal{M}}}^{W}_{\Gamma_{0,B,I}^{\text{labeled}}}.

As before, we add the superscript ‘labeled’ since in most of the article we shall assume that the boundary points are marked by
\emptyset
, but it is sometimes more convenient to assume that they are marked bijectively by
[k]\subset\Omega
.
As in Notation
1.45
, when
B=[k],I=[l]
we similarly define
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{0,k,l}
,
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{0,k,l}^{\text{labeled}}
,

{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{0,k,l}^{W}
, and
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{0,k,l}^{W,\text{labeled}}
.
We also write

\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,\{B_{J}\}_{J\subseteq{[a]}},\{\vec{a}_{j}\}_{j\in[l]}}^{W},~\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,\{k_{J}\}_{J\subseteq{[a]}},\{\vec{a}_{j}\}_{j\in[l]}}^{W}
for
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{\Gamma_{0,\{B_{J}\}_{J\subseteq{[a]}},\{\vec{a}_{j}\}_{j\in[l]}}^{W}}^{W},~{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{\Gamma_{0,\{k_{J}\}_{J\subseteq{[a]}},\{\vec{a}_{j}\}_{j\in[l]}}^{W}}^{W},
respectively, and
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,\{B_{J}\}_{J\subseteq{[a]}},\{\vec{a}_{j}\}_{j\in[l]}}^{W,\text{labeled}},~\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,\{k_{J}\}_{J\subseteq{[a]}},\{\vec{a}_{j}\}_{j\in[l]}}^{W,\text{labeled}}
for their labeled versions. When
W=x^{r}
we write the superscript ‘
1/r
’ instead of ‘
W
.’ When
B_{\emptyset}=\emptyset
or
k_{\emptyset}=0
, we omit it from the notation.

Remark 2.3
.

The labeled versions of the moduli spaces will only be used in this section, mainly in Proposition
2.4
and in
§
2.5
to create a consistent family of orientations on our moduli space.

The moduli space
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,k,l}^{\textup{labeled}}
of stable marked disks is considered in
[
42
]
. It is a smooth orientable manifold with corners in the sense of
[
29
]
, and its dimension is

(2.2)

\dim_{\mathbb{R}}(\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,k,l}^{\textup{labeled}})=\dim_{\mathbb{R}}(\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,k,l})=k+2l-3.

There is a set-theoretic decomposition analogous to (
2.1
),

(2.3)

\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,k,l}^{W,\textup{labeled}}=\bigsqcup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\{\vec{a}_{j}\}_{j\in[l]},\ \{k_{J}\}_{J\subseteq{[a]}},\\
\sum_{J\subseteq{[a]}}k_{J}=k\end{subarray}}\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,\{k_{J}\}_{J\subseteq{[a]}},\{\vec{a}_{j}\}_{j\in[l]}}^{W,\textup{labeled}}.

If we set
k=\sum k_{T}
, then there is a forgetful map

\text{For}_{\text{\text{spin}}}:\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,\{k_{J}\}_{J\subseteq{[a]}},(\vec{a}_{j})_{j\in[l]}}^{W,\textup{labeled}}\rightarrow\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,k,l}^{\textup{labeled}},

or more generally

\text{For}_{\text{\text{spin}}}:\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{\Gamma}^{W}\rightarrow\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{\text{for}_{\text{spin}}(\Gamma)},

which forgets the spin structures and removes the orbifold structure at
the special points. By
Proposition
1.26
,
if the domain is non-empty, this map is a bijection at the level of
sets. We use these bijections to give
the coarse moduli space of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,k,l}^{W,\textup{labeled}}
the structure of a manifold with corners.

This describes the underlying coarse moduli space of

\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{\Gamma}^{W}
. We now explain a procedure that defines an orbifold with corners structure on
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{\Gamma}^{W}
, in the sense of
[
38
, Section 3]
.

Proposition 2.4
.

Let
\Gamma
be a smooth pre-graded
W
-spin graph.
There exists a compact moduli space
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{\Gamma}^{W}
of stable pre-graded
W
-spin disks whose dual graph smooths to
\Gamma
.
It is a smooth orbifold with corners of real dimension
|T^{B}(\Gamma)|+2|T^{I}(\Gamma)|-3|{{\textup{Conn}}}(\Gamma)|
. Its universal bundle admits a universal
r_{i}
-spin grading for each
i\in[a]
.

Proof.

We first establish the result in the case of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,k,l}^{W,\text{labeled}}
.
What we do is completely analogous to
[
5
]
, §3.2 (which, in turn, follows the procedure performed in
[
38
, Section 2]
, and sketched also in Section 1 of
[
38
]
). We have the following sequence of maps, with arrows
labeled by the step in which they are defined below:

(2.4)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,k,l}^{W,\textup{labeled}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathcal{M}}_{0,k,l}^{W,\textup{labeled}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0,k,l}^{W,\textup{labeled}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0,k,l}^{W,\mathbb{Z}_{2},\textup{labeled}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,k+2l}^{W,\mathbb{Z}_{2},\textup{labeled}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,k+2l}^{{}^{\prime}W,\textup{labeled}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,k,l}^{W}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(E)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(F)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(D)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(C)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(B)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(A)}

Let us now define the moduli spaces appearing in (
2.4
). We will go through them in reverse order.

Step (A):
The morphism
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,k+2l}^{W,\mathbb{Z}_{2},\textup{labeled}}\rightarrow\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,k+2l}^{{}^{\prime}W,\textup{labeled}}
. The space
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,k+2l}^{{}^{\prime}W,\textup{labeled}}
is the sub-orbifold of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,k+2l}^{W}
given by the conditions

(1)

The first
k
markings,
w_{1},\ldots,w_{k}
, have twists which satisfy

\forall j\in[k],i\in[a],~\text{tw}_{i}(w_{j})\in\{0,r_{i}-2\}.

(2)

For every
i\in[a]

\frac{\sum_{j\in[k+2l]}\text{tw}_{i}(w_{j})-(r_{i}-2)}{r_{i}}\equiv-1+\sum_{T:i\in T}k_{T}\mod 2.

The latter condition comes from (
1.22
).
Inside this space,
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,k+2l}^{W,\mathbb{Z}_{2},\textup{labeled}}
is the fixed locus of the involution defined by

(C;w_{1},\ldots,w_{k+2l},\{S_{i}\})\mapsto(\overline{C};w_{1},\ldots,w_{k},w_{k+l+1},\ldots,w_{k+2l},w_{k+1},\ldots,w_{k+l},\{\overline{S}_{i}\}),

where
\overline{C}
,
\overline{S}_{i},~i\in[a]
are the same as
C
,
S_{i},~i\in[a]
, respectively, but with the conjugate complex structure.
As the moduli space
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,k+2l}^{W,\mathbb{Z}_{2},\textup{labeled}}
is
the fixed locus of the above anti-holomorphic involution, it
has the structure of a real orbifold. A point in the fixed locus comes equipped with an involution
\phi:C\rightarrow C
given by conjugation which is covered by involutions
\tilde{\phi}_{i}:S_{i}\rightarrow S_{i}
for all
i
.
This moduli space parameterizes isomorphism types of marked spin spheres with a real structure, involutions
\phi
,
\tilde{\phi}_{i},~i\in[a]
, and the prescribed twists. The moduli space
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,k+2l}^{W,\mathbb{Z}_{2},\textup{labeled}}
then maps to
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,k+2l}^{{}^{\prime}W,\textup{labeled}}
, so it inherits a universal curve via pullback.
Note that in general
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,k+2l}^{W,\mathbb{Z}_{2},\textup{labeled}}
is not a sub-orbifold of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,k+2l}^{{}^{\prime}W,\textup{labeled}}
as isotropy is lost: We now cannot scale the
r
-spin structure by an arbitrary
r
th root of unity as its action must be the same under conjugation. This means that the only scaling that can happen is by
\pm 1
in the case where
r
is even.

Step (B):
The morphism
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0,k,l}^{W,\mathbb{Z}_{2},\textup{labeled}}\rightarrow\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,k+2l}^{W,\mathbb{Z}_{2},\textup{labeled}}
.
Take
N\hookrightarrow\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,k+2l}^{W,\mathbb{Z}_{2},\textup{labeled}}
to be the real simple normal crossing divisor consisting of
curves with at least one boundary node.
Via the real hyperplane blowup of

[
38
]
, we cut
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,k+2l}^{W,\mathbb{Z}_{2},\textup{labeled}}
along
N
, yielding an orbifold with corners
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0,k,l}^{W,\mathbb{Z}_{2},\textup{labeled}}
. The morphism in this step is then constructed by gluing the cuts described here.

Step (C):
The morphism
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0,k,l}^{W,\textup{labeled}}\rightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0,k,l}^{W,\mathbb{Z}_{2},\textup{labeled}}
. From here, we define
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0,k,l}^{W,\textup{labeled}}
to be the disconnected
2
-to-
1
cover of
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0,k,l}^{W,\mathbb{Z}_{2},\textup{labeled}}
. The generic point of the moduli space
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0,k,l}^{W,\textup{labeled}}
corresponds to a smooth marked real spin sphere with a choice of a distinguished connected disk component of
C\setminus C^{\phi}
. Equivalently, in the generic (smooth) situation, we are choosing an orientation for
C^{\phi}
. It is important to note, however, that this choice can be uniquely continuously extended to nodal points, see
[
38
]
, §2.6,
as opposed to being chosen independently for each boundary component.

Step (D):
The morphism
\widehat{\mathcal{M}}_{0,k,l}^{W,\textup{labeled}}\hookrightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0,k,l}^{W,\textup{labeled}}
. Inside
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0,k,l}^{W,\textup{labeled}}
, we denote by
\widehat{\mathcal{M}}_{0,k,l}^{W,\textup{labeled}}
the union of connected components such that the marked points points
w_{k+1},\ldots,w_{k+l}
lie in the distinguished stable disk and, for even
r_{i},
the
i^{th}
spin structure is compatible in the sense of Definition
1.21
. The morphism here is inclusion.

Step (E):
The morphism
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,k,l}^{W,\textup{labeled}}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathcal{M}}_{0,k,l}^{W,\textup{labeled}}
. Here,
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,k,l}^{W,\textup{labeled}}
is the cover of
\widehat{\mathcal{M}}_{0,k,l}^{W,\textup{labeled}}
given by a choice of lifting. By Proposition
1.25
, the cover is of degree
2^{e}
, where
e
is the number of even
r_{i}
. Thus we have given the moduli space
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,k,l}^{W,\textup{labeled}}
defined
in Definition
2.2
the structure of an orbifold with corners. The proof of this fact is similar to the proof of the analogous claim, Theorem 2, in
[
38
]
, and will be omitted.

Over
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,k,l}^{W,\textup{labeled}}
, there is a universal curve whose fibers are compatible stable
W
-spin disks. Take a component from the decomposition given in Equation (
2.3
), say
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{0,\{k_{J}\}_{J\subseteq[a]},\{\vec{a}_{j}\}_{j\in[l]}}^{W,\textup{labeled}}
. It has forgetful maps, for
each
i\in[a]
,

(2.5)

\text{For}_{\text{spin }\neq i}:{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{0,\{k_{J}\}_{J\subseteq[a]},\{\vec{a}_{j}\}_{j\in[l]}}^{W,\textup{labeled}}\rightarrow{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}^{1/{r_{i}},\textup{labeled}}_{0,\sum_{J|i\in J}k_{J},\{a_{j,i}\}_{j\in[l]}}

which forget all spin structures but the
i^{th}
one, while also forgetting the consequently untwisted boundary points. We define the
r_{i}
-spin lifting
for
i\in[a]
on
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,k,l}^{W,\textup{labeled}}
as the pullbacks with respect to
these maps of the lifting on
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}^{1/{r_{i}},\textup{labeled}}_{0,\sum_{J|i\in J}k_{J},\{a_{j,i}\}_{j\in[l]}}
, as in
[
5
]
, Theorem 3.4.

Step (F):
The morphism
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,k,l}^{W,\textup{labeled}}\rightarrow\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,k,l}^{W}
.
Finally, for any smooth pre-graded
W
-spin graph
\Gamma
, let
\Gamma^{\prime}
be the graph obtained from
\Gamma
by allowing all possible cyclic orders at open vertices, and let
\widetilde{\Gamma}
be the graph obtained from
\Gamma^{\prime}
by replacing the marking functions by injective marking functions. Then
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}^{W}_{\widetilde{\Gamma}}
is the product of moduli spaces of the form
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{0,k,l}^{W,\text{labeled}}
. Finally, the moduli space
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{\Gamma}^{W}
is isomorphic to the union of those connected components of
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{\widetilde{\Gamma}}^{W}/\text{Aut}(\Gamma^{\prime})
whose boundary cyclic orders correspond to those in
\Gamma
. The universal bundles and gradings are defined by pulling back from
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{\widetilde{\Gamma}}^{W}/\text{Aut}(\Gamma^{\prime})
.
∎

Remark 2.5
.

Henceforth, we usually denote a pre-graded or graded
W
-spin disk simply by
\Sigma
, meaning the preferred half along with the spin structure and marked points, suppressing most of the notation. We will write
\Sigma\in\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,k,l}^{W}
for the point
\Sigma
represents in
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,k,l}^{W}
.

2.1.1.
Moduli associated to dual graphs

We also want moduli spaces associated to dual graphs in which we keep track of cyclic orders
on boundary marked points. We define them as follows.

Given a connected stable genus zero twisted
W
-spin pre-graded dual graph
\Gamma
, recall the smoothing
{\text{sm}}\,\Gamma
of
\Gamma
defined in Definition
1.49
. As
{\text{sm}}\,\Gamma
is a smooth connected graph with one vertex, it is associated to a moduli space
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{{\text{sm}}\Gamma}^{W}
as in Proposition
2.4
.

Definition 2.6
.

Given a connected stable genus zero twisted
W
-spin pre-graded dual graph
\Gamma
, there is a closed embedding

(2.6)

\iota_{\Gamma}:\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{W}_{\Gamma}\hookrightarrow\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{W}_{{\text{sm}}\Gamma},

where the general point
\Sigma
of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{W}_{\Gamma}
is a graded open
W
-spin surface with dual graph
\Gamma=\Gamma(\Sigma)
. If
\Gamma
is disconnected, then
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{W}_{\Gamma}
is defined as the product of the moduli spaces
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{W}_{\Gamma_{i}}
associated to its connected components
\Gamma_{i}
.

We have that
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{W}_{\Gamma}
is a closed sub-orbifold with corners of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{W}_{{\text{sm}}\Gamma}
, of (real) codimension

(2.7)

2|E^{I}(\Gamma)|+|E^{B}(\Gamma)|+|H^{CB}(\Gamma)|.

Take
{\mathcal{M}}^{W}_{\Gamma}
to be the open subspace of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{W}_{\Gamma}
consisting of graded
W
-spin disks whose dual graph is
\Gamma
.

Example 2.7
.

Consider the moduli space of disks with one internal point
z_{1}
and three marked boundary points
x_{1},x_{2},x_{3}
. By (
2.2
), we know that the real dimension of
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{0,3,1}
and
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}^{\mathrm{labeled}}_{0,3,1}

is
2
. The labeled moduli has two connected components, depending on the
cyclic order of the boundary marked points. Each connected
component is a hexagon and has
six codimension 1 boundary strata and six codimension 2 boundary strata. We can see that there is no way to have a contracted boundary or internal node in this case. The dimension of each strata, computed using (
2.7
), is
3
minus the number of irreducible components. We draw one of the connected
components of the labeled moduli space in Figure
8
. In this figure, each stratum is accompanied by a depiction of a representative stable marked disk
\Sigma
. Note the unlabeled moduli space is a quotient of
the labeled moduli space by the symmetric group on three letters. This
identifies the two hexagons and further quotients to produce a bigon.
We suppress twists in the example, but they must satisfy the conditions outlined in Definition
1.40
and satisfy Equations (
1.21
) and (
1.22
).

\bullet
z_{1}
\bullet
x_{1}
\bullet
x_{2}
\bullet
x_{3}
\bullet
z_{1}
\bullet
x_{1}
\bullet
x_{2}
\bullet
x_{3}
\bullet
z_{1}
\bullet
x_{3}
\bullet
x_{2}

Figure 8
.
Moduli space of stable disks with 3 boundary markings and one internal marking.

There are forgetful maps between some of the
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{\Gamma}^{W}
. Indeed, we note that marked points can only be forgotten if all their twists are zero, since (
1.17
) must be preserved. Moreover, boundary marked points can only be forgotten if they are in addition non-alternating, since the grading must descend to the moduli space with fewer marked points. With this in mind, we define:

Definition 2.8
.

Given a (not necessarily connected) stable genus zero twisted
W
-spin pre-graded dual graph, we define

\text{For}_{\text{non-alt}}:\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{\Gamma}^{W}\rightarrow\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{\Gamma^{\prime}}^{W}

by forgetting all non-alternating boundary marked points with
\text{tw}=\vec{0}
. However, this may create unstable irreducible components. We repeatedly contract any unstable irreducible component which is not partially stable with an internal
marked point.
This process might create new boundary marked points which were formerly boundary half-nodes. These new boundary marked points may be non-alternating with
\text{tw}=\vec{0}
. In this case, we repeat the process. If the process ends with some unstable connected components which are not partially stable, then we remove them. We update the cyclic orders data in the natural way.

In the case where the graph
\Gamma^{\prime}
is partially stable, we define its moduli to be the moduli of the graph obtained by removing the partially stable irreducible
components. If the resulting graph is empty, we take the moduli space to be a point. If
\Gamma
is a twisted
W
-spin dual graph with a lifting, we analogously define the graph
\Gamma^{\prime}=\text{for}_{\text{non-alt}}(\Gamma)
(which, again, may include partially stable components).

Definition 2.9
.

Let
\Gamma

be a pre-graded
W
-spin graph. The graded
r_{i}
-spin graph
\text{for}_{\text{spin}\neq i}\Gamma
is an
r_{i}
-spin graph with a lifting obtained from
\Gamma
by forgetting the additional
\text{tw}_{j},{\text{alt}}_{j}
data, for
j\neq i
and from forgetting all tails
t
with
(\text{tw}_{i}(t),{\text{alt}}_{i}(t))=(0,0)
.
We obtain a map

\text{For}_{\text{spin}\neq i}:{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{\Gamma}^{W}\to{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}^{1/r_{i}}_{\text{for}_{{\text{spin}\neq i}}\Gamma}

induced by the map (
2.5
).

2.2.
The bundles

2.2.1.
The Witten bundles and their properties

For graded closed
\Gamma
and
i\in[a]
, we define the Witten bundle

\mathcal{W}_{i}=\mathcal{W}_{i,\Gamma}=(R^{1}\pi_{*}\mathcal{S}_{i})^{\vee},

where
\pi:\mathcal{C}\rightarrow\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,n}^{W}
is the universal family and
\mathcal{S}_{i}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}
are the universal twisted spin bundles.

Observation 2.10
.

As stated in Remark
1.14
, we can have at most one marked point with twist
-1
with respect to each
r_{i}
-spin structure, and as a consequence
the degree of each
\mathcal{S}_{i}
is negative. Thus

R^{0}\pi_{*}\mathcal{S}_{i}=0
and hence
(R^{1}\pi_{*}\mathcal{S}_{i})^{\vee}

is a bundle.
This observation, which is crucial for the normalization operation defined below, explains our choice of allowable twist in conditions (
iii
) and (
vii
) of Definition
1.40
.
We note that if such a point exists, it must be the anchor, and whether or not
the point has twist
-1
for the
i^{th}
spin bundle will depend on
i
.

These are complex vector bundles with fibers

H^{1}(C,S_{i})^{\vee}\cong H^{0}(C,J_{i}),

of complex ranks

(2.8)

\frac{\sum_{j\in I}\text{tw}_{i}(z_{j})-(r_{i}-2)}{r_{i}}.

Equivalently, the bundle
\mathcal{W}_{i}
can be constructed by pulling back the Witten bundle with respect to the
r_{i}
-spin structure via the map
\text{For}_{\text{spin}\neq i}
from Definition
2.9
.
Write

\mathcal{W}=\mathcal{W}_{\Gamma}=\bigoplus_{i\in[a]}\mathcal{W}_{i,\Gamma}.

Following
[
5
]
, we now define the open Witten bundles. First, recalling the sequence of maps in (
2.4
), we can construct the corresponding unlabeled versions by quotienting by the appropriate automorphisms, as dictated in Step (F) of Proposition
2.4
:

(2.9)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,k,l}^{W,\textup{labeled}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathcal{M}}_{0,k,l}^{W,\textup{labeled}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0,k,l}^{W,\textup{labeled}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0,k,l}^{W,\mathbb{Z}_{2},\textup{labeled}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,k+2l}^{W,\mathbb{Z}_{2},\textup{labeled}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,k+2l}^{{}^{\prime}W,\textup{labeled}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,k,l}^{W}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathcal{M}}_{0,k,l}^{W}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0,k,l}^{W}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{0,k,l}^{W,\mathbb{Z}_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,k+2l}^{W,\mathbb{Z}_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,k+2l}^{{}^{\prime}W}}

We denote by
\varpi:\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,k,l}^{W}\to\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,k+2l}^{W,\mathbb{Z}_{2}}
the map given by composing the appropriate maps in (
2.9
). Abusing notation somewhat, denote by
\pi:\mathcal{C}\rightarrow\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{W,\mathbb{Z}_{2}}_{0,k+2l}
the universal curve over the moduli space of real spin spheres defined above (after setting the markings of the first
k
points to
\emptyset
), and by
\mathcal{S}_{i}\rightarrow\mathcal{C},~i\in[a],
the universal spin bundles. There are universal involutions

\phi:\mathcal{C}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}

and

\widetilde{\phi}_{i}:\mathcal{S}_{i}\rightarrow\mathcal{S}_{i},

which induce an involution on
R^{1}\pi_{*}\mathcal{S}_{i}
.
Set
\mathcal{J}_{i}:=\mathcal{S}^{\vee}_{i}\otimes\omega_{\pi}
. Define

\mathcal{W}^{\text{pre}}_{i}:=(R^{0}\pi_{*}\mathcal{J}_{i})_{+}=(R^{1}\pi_{*}\mathcal{S}_{i})^{\vee}_{-}

to be the real vector bundle of
\widetilde{\phi}_{i}
-invariant sections of
J_{i}
. Note here the second equality uses Serre duality, under which invariant sections become anti-invariant.
The real rank of
\mathcal{W}^{\text{pre}}_{i}
is seen in Equation (4.2) of
[
5
]
to be

(2.10)

\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{2l}\text{tw}_{i}(z_{j})+(-1+\sum_{T:i\in T}k_{T})(r_{i}-2)}{r_{i}}.

Definition 2.11
.

The
open Witten bundles
are defined on
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{W}_{0,k,l}
by
\mathcal{W}_{i}=\varpi^{*}\mathcal{W}^{\text{pre}}_{i}
.
For an open
\Gamma,
let
\iota_{\Gamma}:{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{\Gamma}\to{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{{\text{sm}}\Gamma}

be the embedding as described in Definition
2.6
. We write
\mathcal{W}_{i,\Gamma}=\iota_{\Gamma}^{*}\mathcal{W}_{i,{\text{sm}}\Gamma}
and

\mathcal{W}_{\Gamma}=\bigoplus_{i\in[a]}\mathcal{W}_{i,\Gamma}.

Let
\Gamma

be a pre-graded
W
-spin graph.
There is an identification of Witten bundles under the map
\text{For}_{\text{non-alt}}
that forgets all non-alternating boundary marked points with
\text{tw}=\vec{0}
. We will use the following observation extensively:

Observation 2.12
.

If
\Gamma^{\prime}
is obtained from
\Gamma
by forgetting all tails
t
with
(\text{tw}(t),{\text{alt}}(t))=(\vec{0},\vec{0}),

then

(2.11)

\text{For}_{\text{non-alt}}^{*}(\mathcal{W}_{\Gamma^{\prime}})\backsimeq\mathcal{W}_{\Gamma}

where
\text{For}_{\text{non-alt}}:{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}^{W}_{\Gamma}\to{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}^{W}_{\Gamma^{\prime}}
is
defined in Definition
2.8
. See
[
5
]
, (4.3).

Applying this in the
r_{i}
-spin case, we thus have a canonical isomorphism

\text{For}_{\text{spin}\neq i}^{*}(\mathcal{W}_{i,\text{for}_{{\text{spin}\neq i}}\Gamma})\backsimeq\mathcal{W}_{i,\Gamma}

where
\text{For}_{\text{spin}\neq i}
is defined in Definition

2.9
.
Here, the graph
\text{for}_{{\text{spin}\neq i}}\Gamma
is the graded
r_{i}
-spin graph obtained from
\Gamma
by
first forgetting the data
\text{tw}_{j},{\text{alt}}_{j}
for
j\neq i,
and second forgetting the boundary tails
h
with
\text{tw}_{i}(h)={\text{alt}}_{i}(h)=0
.

Another important property is the behaviour of the Witten bundles under restriction to strata. We follow §4.2 of
[
5
]
(although these properties are well-known already from Witten’s original work
[
51
]
).

Let
\Gamma
be a pre-graded
W
-spin graph, and let
\widehat{\Gamma}=\textup{detach}_{N}(\Gamma)
for some set
N\subset E(\Gamma)\cup H^{CB}(\Gamma)
of edges and contracted boundary tails, see Definition
1.52
.
Unlike the moduli space of disks, the moduli space of
W
-spin disks does not always have a gluing map
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{\Gamma}}^{W}\rightarrow\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,k,l}^{W}
, because there is no canonical way to glue the fibers of
S_{i},~i\in[a]
at the internal nodes. Instead, we consider the following diagram of morphisms:

(2.12)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{{\textup{for}}_{\text{spin}}(\widehat{\Gamma})}\times_{\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{{\textup{for}}_{\text{spin}}(\Gamma)}}\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{\Gamma}^{W}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{\Gamma}^{W}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,k,l}^{W}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{\Gamma}}^{W}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{\Gamma}}

Here,
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{{\textup{for}}_{\text{spin}}(\Gamma)}\subset\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,k,l}
is the moduli space of marked disks
with dual graph
\Gamma
, and
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{{\textup{for}}_{\text{spin}}(\widehat{\Gamma})}
is analogously defined. Recalling (
1.18
), we can define the morphism
q
to send the spin structure
S_{j},~j\in[a]
to

(2.13)

\widehat{S}_{j}={\nu}^{*}S_{j}\otimes\mathcal{O}\left(-\sum_{p\in{\mathcal{R}}_{j}}[p]\right),

where
{\nu}:\widehat{C}\rightarrow C
is the normalization map and
{\mathcal{R}}_{j}
is the subset of all half-nodes
p
of multiplicity
0
with respect to the
r_{j}
-spin structure which are not anchors in the normalization.
Note that
\widehat{S}_{j}
then has the correct twists for
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{\Gamma}}^{W}
. In particular, it has
\text{tw}_{j}=r_{j}-1
at the Ramond half-nodes corresponding to half-edges in
{\mathcal{R}}_{j}
and
\text{tw}_{j}=-1
at the other Ramond half-nodes.

Since the map
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{{\textup{for}}_{\text{spin}}(\hat{\Gamma})}\to\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{{\textup{for}}_{\text{spin}}(\Gamma)}
is an isomorphism, the projection map
\mu
is an isomorphism. We will distinguish however between the domain and codomain of
\mu
because they have different universal objects. We note that
q
is not an isomorphism, in general. Indeed, the automorphism groups of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{\Gamma}^{W}
and
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{\hat{\Gamma}}^{W}
differ when one computes using Observation
1.15
. However, in the strata of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{\Gamma}
where
\Gamma
has no internal edges, we have that
q
is an isomorphism when restricted to the strata (see
[
5
, Remark 4.5]
). It may be of some comfort to the reader for us to remark that this is an isomorphism on the level of coarse moduli spaces.

Notation 2.13
.

For
\Gamma,N
as above, we denote the map
q\circ\mu^{-1}:\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{\Gamma}^{W}\to\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{\Gamma}}^{W}
by
{{\textup{Detach}}}_{N}
. When
N=\{e\}
is a singleton, we denote this map by
{{\textup{Detach}}}_{e}
.

There are two natural universal curves over the fiber product
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{{\textup{for}}_{\text{spin}}(\widehat{\Gamma})}\times_{\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{{\textup{for}}_{\text{spin}}(\Gamma)}}\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{\Gamma}^{W}
: we define
\mathcal{C}_{\Gamma}
by the fiber diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}_{\Gamma}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{{\textup{for}}_{\text{spin}}(\widehat{\Gamma})}\times_{\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{{\textup{for}}_{\text{spin}}(\Gamma)}}\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{\Gamma}^{W}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,k,l}^{W}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{\Gamma}\circ\mu}

and
\mathcal{C}_{\widehat{\Gamma}}
by the fiber diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}_{\widehat{\Gamma}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathcal{C}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{{\textup{for}}_{\text{spin}}(\widehat{\Gamma})}\times_{\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{{\textup{for}}_{\text{spin}}(\Gamma)}}\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{\Gamma}^{W}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{W}_{\widehat{\Gamma}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{\pi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}

in which
\widehat{\mathcal{C}}
is the universal curve over
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{\Gamma}}^{W}
. There are universal bundles
\mathcal{S}_{j}
and
\widehat{\mathcal{S}}_{j},~j\in[a]
on these two universal curves, and a universal normalization morphism

{\nu}:\mathcal{C}_{\widehat{\Gamma}}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}_{\Gamma}

relating them via (
1.8
). Let
\mathcal{J}_{i}
be the universal twisted spin bundle corresponding to the twisted spin bundle
J_{i}
for
i\in[a]
.

We can now state the decomposition properties of the Witten bundle. We state the properties in the case where
N=\{e\}
for a single edge
e
, but all can be readily generalized to the setting where more than one edge is detached.

Proposition 2.14
.

Let
\Gamma
be a pre-graded
W
-spin graph.
Suppose that
\Gamma
has a single edge
e
, so the general point of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{\Gamma}^{W}
is a stable
W
-spin disk with two components
C_{1}
and
C_{2}
meeting at a node
p
. Let
\widehat{\Gamma}=\textup{detach}_{e}(\Gamma)
. Let
\mathcal{W}_{i}
and
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{i}
denote the
i^{th}
Witten bundle summand on
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,k,l}^{W}
and
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{\Gamma}}^{W}
, respectively.

The Witten bundle decomposes as follows along the node
p
:

(i)

If
e
is Neveu–Schwarz for the
i^{th}
bundle
S_{i}
,
then
\mu^{*}\iota_{\Gamma}^{*}\mathcal{W}_{i}=q^{*}\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{i}
.

(ii)

If
e
is a Ramond boundary edge for the
i^{th}
bundle
S_{i}
, then there is an exact sequence

(2.14)

0\rightarrow\mu^{*}\iota_{\Gamma}^{*}\mathcal{W}_{i}\rightarrow q^{*}\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{i}\rightarrow{\mathcal{T}}_{+}\rightarrow 0,

where
{\mathcal{T}}_{+}
is a trivial real line bundle.

(iii)

If
e
is a Ramond internal edge for the
i^{th}
bundle
S_{i}
, then it connects two vertices corresponding to components
\mathcal{C}_{1}
and
\mathcal{C}_{2}
. Either both components are closed or exactly one is open. Without loss of generality, let
\mathcal{C}_{1}
be the component which is open or the closed component containing the anchor of
\Gamma
. Write
q^{*}\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{i}=\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{i}^{1}\boxplus\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{i}^{2}
, where
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{i}^{1}
is the
i^{th}
Witten summand on the component
\mathcal{C}_{1}
, defined using the spin bundle
\widehat{\mathcal{S}}_{i}|_{\mathcal{C}_{1}}
, and
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{i}^{2}
is the
i^{th}
Witten summand on the component
\mathcal{C}_{2}
. Then:

(a)

There is an exact sequence

(2.15)

0\rightarrow\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{i}^{2}\rightarrow\mu^{*}\iota_{\Gamma}^{*}\mathcal{W}_{i}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{i}^{1}\rightarrow 0.

(b)

If
\widehat{\Gamma}^{\prime}
is defined to agree with
\widehat{\Gamma}
except that the twist at each tail which corresponds to a half-edge of

e
is
r_{i}-1
,
and
q^{\prime}:\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{{\textup{for}}_{\textup{spin}}(\widehat{\Gamma})}\times_{\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{{\textup{for}}_{\textup{spin}}(\Gamma)}}\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{\Gamma}^{W}\rightarrow\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{\Gamma}^{\prime}}^{W}
is defined analogously to
q
, then there is an exact sequence

(2.16)

0\rightarrow\mu^{*}\iota_{\Gamma}^{*}\mathcal{W}_{i}\rightarrow(q^{\prime})^{*}\widehat{\mathcal{W}}^{\prime}_{i}\rightarrow{\mathcal{T}}\rightarrow 0,

where
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}^{\prime}_{i}
is the
i^{\text{th}}
Witten bundle associated to
\widehat{\Gamma}^{\prime}
and
{\mathcal{T}}
is a line bundle whose
r^{\text{th}}
power is trivial.

(iv)

Suppose that
\Gamma
has a single vertex, no edges, and a contracted boundary tail
t
, and let
\widehat{\Gamma}=\textup{detach}_{t}(\Gamma)
. If
\mathcal{W}_{i}
and
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{i}
denote the
i^{th}
Witten summands on
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,k,l}^{W}
and
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{\Gamma}}^{W}
, respectively, then the sequence (
2.14
) also holds in this case.

This is Proposition 4.7 in
[
5
]
. It should be stressed that in the presence of internal edges these decomposition properties do not respect the automorphism group actions on the Witten bundles. Despite this subtlety, a multisection on
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}
canonically induces a multisection of
\mathcal{W}
, see Remark 2.7 in
[
6
]
.

2.2.2.
The tautological lines and direct sums of bundles

For each
i\in I
, a cotangent line bundle
\mathbb{L}_{i}
is defined on the moduli space of stable marked disks as the line bundle whose fiber over
(C,\phi,\Sigma,\{z_{i}\},\{x_{j}\},m^{I},m^{B})
is the cotangent line
T^{*}_{z_{i}}\Sigma
at the interior marked point
z_{i}
.
We define cotangent line bundles
\mathbb{L}_{i}
on
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{W}_{0,k,l}
by pullback under the morphism forgetting the graded spin structure, and, for any graded graph
\Gamma
, we let
\mathbb{L}_{i}^{\Gamma}
be the pullback of
\mathbb{L}_{i}
to
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{\Gamma}
.

The following important observations are discussed further in
[
42
, Section 3.5]
:

Observation 2.15
.

Let
\Gamma
be a pre-graded
W
-spin graph.

(i)

If
i
is a marking of an internal tail of
\Lambda\in{{\textup{Conn}}}(\textup{detach}(\Gamma))
,

\mathbb{L}_{i}^{\Gamma}=\pi^{*}\mathbb{L}_{i}^{\Lambda},

where
\pi:{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}^{W}_{\Gamma}\rightarrow{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}^{W}_{\Lambda}
is the
composition of the map
{{\textup{Detach}}}_{E(\Gamma)}:{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}^{W}_{\Gamma}\rightarrow{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}^{W}_{\textup{detach}(\Gamma)}
of Notation
2.13

with the projection to the factor
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}^{W}_{\Lambda}
.

(ii)

If
\Gamma^{\prime}=\text{for}_{\text{non-alt}}(\Gamma)
, then there exists a canonical morphism

t_{\Gamma}:\text{For}_{\text{non-alt}}^{*}\mathbb{L}_{i}^{\Gamma^{\prime}}\rightarrow\mathbb{L}_{i}^{\Gamma}.

This morphism vanishes identically on the strata
where the component containing
z_{i}
is contracted by the forgetful map. Away
from these strata, this morphism is an isomorphism. See Observations 3.31 and 3.32 of
[
42
]
.

(iii)

\mathbb{L}_{i}^{\Gamma}
is canonically oriented as a complex orbifold line bundle.

Definition 2.16
.

Let
\mathbf{d}=(d_{i})_{i\in I}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{I}
be a vector labeled by a set
I\subseteq\Omega
and let
\Gamma
be a graded graph with
I(\Gamma)\subseteq I
. We write

E_{\Gamma}(\mathbf{d}):=\mathcal{W}_{\Gamma}\oplus\bigoplus_{i\in I(\Gamma)}{\mathbb{L}}_{i}^{\oplus d_{i}}.

We call this the
descendent-Witten bundle with respect to
\mathbf{d}
.
We omit
\Gamma
from the notation if it is clear from context. We call the
\mathcal{W}_{i,\Gamma}
the
Witten bundle components
, while the other summands are called the
tautological line components
.
9
9

9

We remark that

\mathbf{d}
contains data which may be irrelevant for the definition
of
E_{\Gamma}(\mathbf{d})
, i.e.,
d_{i}
for
i\in I\setminus I(\Gamma)
. However,
this will prove to be convenient to avoid even heavier notation in proofs.

The decomposition properties of the bundles
E_{\Gamma}(\mathbf{d})
are easily deduced from the decomposition properties of the Witten bundles and of the tautological line given in Proposition
2.14
and Observation
2.15
.

2.2.3.
Multisections and the relative Euler class

We start by recalling some notation involving multisections of
orbifold bundles. Let
E
be an orbifold vector bundle over an orbifold with corners
M
. We refer the reader to Appendix A of
[
6
]
for more details.

Definition 2.17

(Definition A.1 of
[
6
]
)
.

A
multisection
of
E\to M
is a function

\mathbf{s}:E\to\mathbb{Q}_{\geq 0}

that satisfies the following:

(1)

the function
\mathbf{s}
is well-defined with respect to the orbifold structure on
E
.

(2)

for each
x\in M
, there is an (étale) open neighborhood
U
, a nonempty finite set of smooth local sections
s_{i}:U\to E,i=1,\dots,N
and a collection of positive rational numbers
\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{N}
so that for all
y\in U
,

\sum_{v\in E_{y}}\mathbf{s}(y,v)=1.

and for all
v\in E_{y}

\mathbf{s}(y,v)=\sum_{i,s_{i}(y)=v}\mu_{i}.

We call
s_{i}
the
local branches
and
\mu_{i}
the
weights
.

Note that in a chart where
N=1
, then the weight is
\mu_{1}=1
and the support of
\mathbf{s}(y,v)
is the graph of the local branch
s_{1}
as a section in
E
.

Notation 2.18
.

Given
E
and
M
as above, we denote by
C^{\infty}_{m}(M,E)
the space of smooth multisections. When the base space
M
is understood from the context, we will drop it and write
C^{\infty}_{m}(E)
. If
U\subseteq M
, we will abuse notation and write
C^{\infty}_{m}(U,E)
or
C^{\infty}_{m}(E|_{U})
, rather than a pullback. In words, when we discuss a multisection on a subspace of the base then we mean the total space of the bundle restricted to the subspace of the base. When we do this, the bundle should be obvious from the context. For example, if we have a multisection
\mathbf{s}\in C_{m}^{\infty}(M,E)
and
U\subseteq M
, we will write
\mathbf{s}|_{U}
for
\mathbf{s}|_{E|_{U}}
to compress notation.

There are natural operations for addition of multisections and a multiplication of a multisection by a function. Let
\lambda\in C^{\infty}(M,\mathbb{R})
be a real-valued smooth function and
\mathbf{s},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}\in C_{m}^{\infty}(M,E)
be multisections given locally by
(s_{i},\mu_{i})_{i=1}^{N},~~(s^{\prime}_{i},\mu^{\prime}_{i})_{i=1}^{N^{\prime}},
respectively.
The multisection
\lambda\mathbf{s}
is obtained from
\mathbf{s}
by replacing each
s_{i}
with
\lambda s_{i}
, and keeping
\mu_{i}
the same.
The multisection
\mathbf{s}+\mathbf{s}^{\prime}
is given locally by the collection of smooth local sections
\tilde{s}_{ij}=s_{i}+s^{\prime}_{j}
and
\tilde{\mu}_{ij}=\mu_{i}\mu_{j}
(see
[
42
, Definition A.9]
or
[
6
, Appendix A]
).

Definition 2.19
.

Let
\mathbf{s}_{1},\ldots,\mathbf{s}_{m}
be multisections of an orbifold bundle
E\to M
and take positive integers
a_{1},\ldots,a_{m}\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
.
Define the multisection
\uplus_{i=1}^{m}a_{i}\mathbf{s}_{i}(x,v)
by

\uplus_{i=1}^{m}a_{i}\mathbf{s}_{i}(x,v)=\frac{1}{\sum_{i=1}^{m}a_{i}}\sum a_{i}\mathbf{s}_{i}(x,v).

We note that the support of
\uplus_{i=1}^{m}\mathbf{s}_{i}(x)
is the properly weighted union of the supports of
\mathbf{s}_{i}
. Observe (or recall from Equation (A.2) of
[
6
]
) that

(2.17)

\uplus_{i=1}^{m}\mathbf{s}=\mathbf{s}.

Let
p\in M
and consider the smooth local sections
s_{i}
of
\mathbf{s}
near
p
. If
p\in Z(s_{i})
for some
i
, then we say that
\mathbf{s}

vanishes
at
p
. Otherwise, we say
\mathbf{s}
is nonvanishing at
p
. Given a multisection
\mathbf{s}
the
zero locus
is defined to be
Z(\mathbf{s}):=\{p\in M\ |\ \mathbf{s}\text{ vanishes at $p$}\}
. A multisection
\mathbf{s}
is
transverse to zero
if, for any point
x
in the zero locus of
\mathbf{s}
, there is a neighborhood
U
so that every local branch
s_{i}
of
\mathbf{s}
in
U
is transverse to the zero section, denoted
\mathbf{s}\pitchfork 0
.

Given a global multisection
\mathbf{s}
of
E
on
M
with isolated zeros, one can define the weighted cardinality of its zero set as follows. In a neighborhood of a point
p\in M
, consider the local branches
s_{i}
with weights
\mu_{i}
for
i=1,\ldots,N
of the multisection
\mathbf{s}
. Let
\deg_{p}(s_{i})
be the degree of vanishing of
s_{i}
at
p
(see Appendix A of
[
6
]
for a precise definition). Note that this is zero if
s_{i}(p)\neq 0
. We then can say the weight of
\mathbf{s}
at
p
is given by

(2.18)

\epsilon_{p}:=\frac{1}{|G|}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mu_{i}\deg_{p}(s_{i})

where
G
is the isotropy group of the orbifold at
p
. Then we can define the weighted cardinality of the zero locus
\#Z(\mathbf{s})
of
\mathbf{s}
to be

(2.19)

\#Z(\mathbf{s})=\sum_{p\in M}\epsilon_{p}.

We emphasize that both the zero locus (and the weighted cardinality of the zero locus) are (weighted) subsets of the base space
M
.

We now define the relative Euler class as constructed in Appendix A and Notation 3.2 of
[
42
]
.
By Theorem A.14 of
[
42
]
, if (i)
\dim M=\operatorname{rank}E
,
(ii)
\mathbf{s}
a nowhere vanishing multisection of
E|_{\partial M}
, and
(iii)
\tilde{\mathbf{s}}\in C_{m}^{\infty}(E)
is a transverse extension of
\mathbf{s}
to all of
M
, then
the weighted cardinality of the zero set
\#Z(\tilde{\mathbf{s}})
depends only on
\mathbf{s}
and not on the choice of
\tilde{\mathbf{s}}
. For this reason, we will often conflate notation and denote a choice of extension
\tilde{\mathbf{s}}
just by
\mathbf{s}
. Moreover, this means that the homology class
[Z(\tilde{\mathbf{s}})]\in H_{0}(M,\mathbb{Q})
only depends on
E
and
\mathbf{s}
.
Note that this can be extended to a special case of noncompact orbifolds
M
in the sense that one can replace
\partial M
with
U\cup\partial M
where
M\setminus(U\cup\partial M)
is compact (see Appendix A of
[
6
]
).

Definition 2.20
.

Let
E
be an orbifold vector bundle over an orbifold with corners
M
. Take an open sub-orbifold
U\subseteq M
such that
M\setminus U
is a compact orbifold with corners. Given a nowhere vanishing smooth multisection
\mathbf{s}\in C^{\infty}_{m}(E|_{\partial M\cup U})
, we denote by
e(E;\mathbf{s})\in H^{*}(M,\partial M\cup U)
the Poincaré dual of the zero locus
[Z(\tilde{\mathbf{s}})]\in H_{0}(M)
of a transverse extension
\tilde{\mathbf{s}}
of
\mathbf{s}
to
M
. We call
e(E;\mathbf{s})

the relative Euler class
of
E
and
\mathbf{s}
.

Notation 2.21
.

Suppose given a covering map
F:M\rightarrow M^{\prime}

of orbifolds with corners with (positive) finite degree
\deg F
. Suppose
also given
orbifold vector bundles
E,E^{\prime}
on
M
and
M^{\prime}
respectively with

E=F^{*}E^{\prime}
. Then for a multisection
\mathbf{s}\in C^{\infty}_{m}(E)
, we may
define a multisection
F_{*}\mathbf{s}
given by

(F_{*}\mathbf{s})(x^{\prime},v^{\prime})={1\over\deg F}\sum_{x\in F^{-1}(x^{\prime})}\mathbf{s}(x,v_{x})

where
v_{x}\in E_{x}
is the point of the fibre
E_{x}
mapping to

v^{\prime}\in E^{\prime}_{x^{\prime}}
.
Note this needs to be interpreted in an orbifold sense. For example, if

M\rightarrow M^{\prime}
is a quotient by a finite group
G
acting trivially
on
M
but possibly non-trivially on
E
, then
F_{*}\mathbf{s}
coincides with

\biguplus_{g\in G}g^{*}\mathbf{s}
.

If
\mathbf{s}
is transverse to the zero-section of
E
, then it is easy to see
that
F_{*}\mathbf{s}
is transverse to the zero-section of
E^{\prime}
, and if
\mathbf{s}
has
a finite number of zeros,

(2.20)

\#Z(F_{*}\mathbf{s})={1\over\deg F}\#Z(\mathbf{s}),\quad\#Z(F^{*}F_{*}\mathbf{s})=\#Z(\mathbf{s}).

Here, the pull-back of a multisection is given
by the standard notion of pull-back of sections of vector bundles.

We finish this subsection with the following useful tool.

Lemma 2.22
.

Let
E\to M
be an orbifold vector bundle over an orbifold with corners, with
\operatorname{rank}(E)=\dim(M)
, and let
U\subseteq M
be an open set with
M\setminus U

compact.
Let
\mathbf{s}_{0}
and
\mathbf{s}_{1}
be nowhere-vanishing smooth multisections on
U\cup\partial M
. Denote by
p:[0,1]\times M\to M
the projection, and let

H\in C_{m}^{\infty}([0,1]\times(U\cup\partial M),p^{*}E)

where
U\subseteq M
is an open set with
M\setminus U
compact.
Suppose
H|_{\{i\}\times E}=\mathbf{s}_{i}
,
H\pitchfork 0
and
H
is nowhere vanishing in
[0,1]\times U
.
Then

\int_{M}e(E;\mathbf{s}_{1})-\int_{M}e(E;\mathbf{s}_{0})=\#Z(H)=\#Z(H|_{[0,1]\times\partial M}).

This lemma is the non-compact orbifold analog of
[
42
, Lemma 3.55]
and the proof is verbatim.

Remark 2.23
.

Note that we orient
[0,1]
by its natural orientation and the boundary of
M
by its induced orientation. We write the unit interval first to denote that its orientation is first in the appropriate ordering of the orientations of the product. This will become important in Subsection
2.5
(see, e.g., Observation
2.41
).

2.3.
Coherent multisections and their assembling

This subsection is technical and a reader trying to understand only the main thrust of the paper may skip it.
As indicated in the introduction, the boundary conditions of interest
will be inductively defined, and lead to the definition of canonical
families of multisections. However, the key topological recursion result
of the paper, Theorem
5.10
, requires understanding the
vanishing of certain sections along strata of the moduli spaces where the
disks have one internal node. Such strata are not actually contained
in the topological boundary of the moduli space, so the behaviour of canonical
sections along these strata is not particularly controlled.
Thus it will be important in the
proof of open topological recursion to choose canonical
multisections also satisfying an inductive structure along these strata:
these sections will be called
special canonical multisections
,
see Definition
3.23
. Ideally one would
like to specify an element of the fibre of the Witten bundle at a point
of the moduli space corresponding to a disk with an internal node
by gluing sections of
\widehat{J}_{1},\widehat{J}_{2}
on the two curves
obtained by normalizing
the internal node. However, in the case of Ramond nodes, the gluing
properties of the Witten bundle as stated in Lemma
2.14

are subtle.
This leads to the technical complication addressed in this subsection.

In fact this notion of special canonical only plays a role in the
proof of Lemma
6.4
, whose proof follows
the proof of the analogous result in
[
6
]
. However, there are
some slight complications in the generalization of the definitions
of coherent multisections from
[
6
]
, and hence we must devote
some space to this concept here, even though the use of this concept
will be largely invisible for the remainder of the paper.

2.3.1.
Coherent multisections

Consider a (possibly disconnected) stable graded

W
-spin dual graph
\Gamma
.
For any twisted
r_{i}
-spin structure on a
W
-spin surface
with dual graph
\Gamma
, we set

J^{\prime}_{i}:=J_{i}\otimes\mathcal{O}\left(\sum_{t}[z_{t}]\right),

where the sum is over all anchors
t
with twist
\text{tw}_{i}(t)=-1
.
Effectively, we are changing the twists of the anchors to
r-1
,
as described in Proposition
2.14
(iii)(b).

Similarly, on the universal curve over
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{\Gamma}^{W}
, we take a modified universal line bundle

\mathcal{J}^{\prime}_{i}:=\mathcal{J}_{i}\otimes\mathcal{O}\left(\sum_{t}\Delta_{z_{t}}\right),

where
\Delta_{z_{t}}
is the divisor in the universal curve corresponding to
z_{t}
.
Define an orbifold vector bundle
{\mathcal{R}}_{\Gamma}
on
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{\Gamma}^{W}
by

{\mathcal{R}}_{\Gamma}=\bigoplus_{i=1}^{a}\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}\text{anchors $t$,}\\
\text{tw}_{i}(t)=-1\end{subarray}}\sigma_{z_{t}}^{*}\mathcal{J}^{\prime}_{i},

where
\sigma_{z_{t}}
is the corresponding section of the universal curve. The second summation only runs over anchors
t
that have
\text{tw}_{i}(t)=-1
for the given
i
.
10
10

10

Consider the special case that all anchors are either (1) tails
which are Neveu-Schwarz
for all
i\in[a]
or (2) contracted boundary tails. Then
{\mathcal{R}}_{\Gamma}
is the rank 0 vector bundle on
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{\Gamma}^{W}
.
If
\Gamma
is connected, then
\mathcal{R}_{\Gamma}
contains at most
one summand for each
i
.
Throughout what follows (except in the current subsection), the notation
{\mathcal{R}}_{\Gamma}
will not be used for graphs that are not closed, to avoid confusion.

We use the notation
{\mathcal{R}}_{\Gamma}
both for the bundle and for its total space, and we denote by

{\mathrm{proj}}:{\mathcal{R}}_{\Gamma}\rightarrow\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{\Gamma}^{W}

the projection.
For any degeneration
\Gamma^{\prime}\in\partial\Gamma
of the graph
\Gamma
, there is a map
{\mathcal{R}}_{\Gamma^{\prime}}\rightarrow{\mathcal{R}}_{\Gamma}
between total spaces, which is an embedding on the underlying coarse level.

We denote
by
\mathcal{W}_{i}^{\prime}
the bundle

{\mathcal{W}}_{i}^{\prime}=(R^{0}\pi_{*}\mathcal{J}_{i}^{\prime})_{+}

on
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{\Gamma}^{W}
, and by
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{W}}_{i}^{\prime}
the pullback of the bundle
(R^{0}\pi_{*}\mathcal{J}_{i}^{\prime})_{+}
to
{\mathcal{R}}_{\Gamma}
via
{\mathrm{proj}}
.

Definition 2.24
.

Let
\Gamma
be a connected graded
W
-spin dual graph, and let
s
be a multisection of
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{W}}^{\prime}_{i}
over a subset
U\subset{\mathcal{R}}_{\Gamma}
for
i\in[a]
. We say that
\mathbf{s}
is
coherent
if either:

(1)

the anchor of
\Gamma
does not have
\text{tw}_{i}=-1
; or

(2)

take any point
\zeta=(\Sigma,\vec{u})\in U
. This consists of
data of a graded
W
-spin surface
\Sigma
with bundles

J_{i}^{\prime}
for each
i
and an element
\vec{u}
of the fibre
of
{\mathcal{R}}_{\Gamma}
over the corresponding point of
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{\Gamma}^{W}
.
The choice of
\vec{u}
is equivalent to the data

\vec{u}_{t}\in\bigoplus_{i\big|\text{tw}_{i}(z_{t})=-1}(J^{\prime}_{i})_{z_{t}}
for
each anchor
t
which has twist
-1
for some
i
. Then, for any local branch
s_{j}
of the multisection
\mathbf{s}
, the element
s_{j}(\zeta)\in H^{0}(\Sigma,J^{\prime}_{i})

satisfies

\textrm{ev}_{z_{t}}s_{j}(\zeta)=(u_{t})_{i}.

Here
\textrm{ev}_{z_{t}}
denotes the value of a section of the bundle

J_{i}^{\prime}
at the point
z_{t}
, and
(u_{t})_{i}
denotes
the component of
\vec{u}_{t}
which corresponds to the
i^{th}
bundle.

A
coherent multisection
of
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{W}}^{\prime}:=\bigoplus_{i=1}^{a}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{W}}^{\prime}_{i}
is a direct
sum of coherent multisections of
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{W}}^{\prime}_{i},i=1,\ldots,a
.

If
\Gamma
is instead a disconnected graded
W
-spin graph, we say that a multisection
\mathbf{s}
of
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{W}}^{\prime}_{i}
or
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{W}}^{\prime}
over
U\subset{\mathcal{R}}_{\Gamma}
is coherent if it can be written as the restriction to
U
of

\boxplus_{\Lambda\in{{\textup{Conn}}}(\Gamma)}\mathbf{s}_{\Lambda},

where each
\mathbf{s}_{\Lambda}
is a coherent multisection of
(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{W}}^{\prime}_{i})_{\Lambda}\rightarrow U_{\Lambda}
or
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{W}}^{\prime}_{\Lambda}\rightarrow U_{\Lambda},
respectively, for some
U_{\Lambda}\subseteq{\mathcal{R}}_{\Lambda}
.

Note that, if
\mathbf{s}
is a coherent multisection of
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{W}}^{\prime}_{i}\rightarrow{\mathcal{R}}_{\Gamma}
and
\zeta=(\Sigma,\vec{0})\in{\mathcal{R}}_{\Gamma}
, the multisection
\mathbf{s}(\zeta)
of
J^{\prime}_{i}
vanishes at each anchor
z_{t}
with twist
-1
, and thus it is induced by a multisection of
J_{i}
. In other words, the restriction of a coherent multisection
\mathbf{s}
to
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{\Gamma}^{W}
is canonically identified with a multisection of
\mathcal{W}_{i}\rightarrow\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{\Gamma}^{W}
, which we will call
\overline{\mathbf{s}}
. If the graded
W
-spin dual graph
\Gamma
has no anchor
z_{t}
with twist
\text{tw}_{i}(t)=-1
for some
i\in[a]
, then
\overline{\mathbf{s}}=\mathbf{s}
.

We note, also, that a multisection of
{\mathrm{proj}}^{*}(\mathcal{W}\to{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{\Gamma}^{W})
induces a multisection of
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{W}}^{\prime}\rightarrow{\mathcal{R}}_{\Gamma}
which vanishes along the
z_{t}
. Adding this multisection to a coherent multisection of
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{W}}^{\prime}\rightarrow{\mathcal{R}}_{\Gamma}
thus yields another coherent multisection.

2.3.2.
The assembling operation

In §4.1.3 of
[
6
]
, the authors define the
assembling operation

. In the
r
-spin case, if
E^{\prime}
is a subset of internal edges of

\Gamma
, the assembling operation takes a coherent multisection of
\mathbf{s}
of
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{W}}^{\prime}
on
{\mathcal{R}}_{\textup{detach}_{E^{\prime}}\Gamma}
and yields a coherent multisection
{{\lx@scalerel@obj{
\hbox to28.45pt{\vbox to28.45pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 14.22638pt\lower-14.22638pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {}{{}}{}

of
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{W}}^{\prime}
on
{\mathcal{R}}_{\Gamma}
.

In our situation, this operation generalizes to coherent multisections of
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{W}}^{\prime}

with an analogous construction.
We refer the reader to §4.1.3 in
[
6
]
for details, only sketching the construction and the slight differences needed to generalize from the
r
-spin
to the
W
-spin case, and then mentioning
the relevant properties for this paper.

We first discuss the assembling operation when

\Gamma
is a connected graded
W
-spin graph and

E^{\prime}=\{e\}
a single internal edge.
Let
\mathbf{s}=\oplus_{i\in[a]}\mathbf{s}_{i}
be a coherent multisection of

\boldsymbol{\mathcal{W}}^{\prime}\to{\mathcal{R}}_{\textup{detach}_{e}\Gamma}
, with
\mathbf{s}_{i}
a coherent
multisection
of
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{W}}^{\prime}_{i}
. Let
\Gamma_{1}
and
\Gamma_{2}
be the connected components of
\textup{detach}_{e}\Gamma
, with half-edges
h_{1}
and
h_{2}
respectively
corresponding to the edge
e
of
\Gamma
. Without loss of generality, we choose
\Gamma_{1}
so that it contains the open vertices or the anchor of
\Gamma
.
Thus
\Gamma_{2}
will contain an additional anchor, the half-edge
h_{2}
.

A point of
{\mathcal{R}}_{\Gamma}
consists of a

W
-spin surface
\Sigma
with dual graph lying in
\partial^{!}\Gamma

along with a choice of some additional data
\vec{u}_{t}
at the anchor
t

of
\Sigma
, if it has one, indexed by the tail
t
.
We obtain a corresponding normalization

\widehat{\Sigma}=\Sigma_{1}\sqcup\Sigma_{2}
. Then
(\Sigma_{1},\vec{u}_{t})

determines a point of
{\mathcal{R}}_{\Gamma_{1}}
and
\Sigma_{2}
determines
a point of
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}^{W}_{\Gamma_{2}}
. In particular, there is a natural morphism

{\mathcal{R}}_{\Gamma}\rightarrow{\mathcal{R}}_{\Gamma_{1}}\times{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{\Gamma_{2}}^{W}
.

Let
z_{1}
,
z_{2}
be the half-nodes of

\Sigma_{1}\cup\Sigma_{2}
corresponding to the half-edges
h_{1}
,
h_{2}
of
\Gamma
.
For each
i
such that
\text{tw}_{i}(h_{2})=-1
,
we choose an identification
\rho_{i}
of
(\mathcal{J}^{\prime}_{1,i})_{z_{1}}
and

(\mathcal{J}_{2,i}^{\prime})_{z_{2}}
; there are
r_{i}
possible choices which differ by

r_{i}^{th}
roots of unity.
11
11

11

One can see this choice does not affect
the result, as the different choices of identification are related by
automorphisms of the
W
-spin curve
\Sigma_{2}
given by rescaling the
spin bundles, see Remark
1.12
.

In any event, we may write
\mathbf{s}_{i}=\mathbf{s}_{i}^{1}\boxplus\mathbf{s}_{i}^{2}
, where

\mathbf{s}_{i}^{1}
and
\mathbf{s}_{i}^{2}
are coherent multisections over
{\mathcal{R}}_{\Gamma_{1}}

and
{\mathcal{R}}_{\Gamma_{2}}
. This allows us to lift the morphism

{\mathcal{R}}_{\Gamma}\rightarrow{\mathcal{R}}_{\Gamma_{1}}\times{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}^{W}_{\Gamma_{2}}

to a morphism
{{\textup{Detach}}}_{e}:{\mathcal{R}}_{\Gamma}\rightarrow{\mathcal{R}}_{\Gamma_{1}}\times{\mathcal{R}}_{\Gamma_{2}}
by taking
(\Sigma,\vec{u}_{t})
to the
pair

\big((\Sigma_{1},\vec{u}_{t}),(\Sigma_{2},(\rho_{i}(\mathbf{s}_{i}^{1}(z_{1})))_{i|\text{tw}_{i}(z_{2})=-1})\big)
.

The

i^{th}
component of the multisection

•

(

). The

{{\lx@scalerel@obj{

is then,
under this identification, the pullback

{{\textup{Detach}}}_{e}^{*}(\mathbf{s}^{1}_{i}\boxplus\mathbf{s}_{i}^{2})
.

•

).

. In particular,

. This gives the

i^{th}

2.4.
Rank two

In the special case
W=x^{r}+y^{s}
, we call a
W
-spin disk an
{(r,s)}
-disk and a
W
-spin graph an
(r,s)
-graph.
In this case, we write the twist vector as
(a,b)
instead of
(a_{1},a_{2})
.

Recall that we have the set
\Omega
used to mark points, containing
a copy of
\mathbb{N}
. We will, from now on, assume given a function

\text{tw}:\mathbb{N}\rightarrow\{0,\ldots,r-1\}\times\{0,\ldots,s-1\}

associating to each
i\in\mathbb{N}\subseteq\Omega
a fixed choice of twist
(a_{i},b_{i})
.
We assume that for each
(a,b)\in\{0,\ldots,r-1\}\times\{0,\ldots,s-1\}
,
the inverse image
\text{tw}^{-1}(a,b)
is infinite.

For a pre-graded
{(r,s)}
-disk,
we call a boundary marked point with
\text{tw}=(r-2,0),{\text{alt}}=(1,0)
an
r
-point. We similarly define an
s
-point. The
r
-points and
s
-points are the two types
of singly twisted (see Definition
1.29
)
points on a pre-graded
{(r,s)}
-spin disk. We shall denote the
r
-points by
x_{i}
and the
s
-points by
y_{j}
.
We use the same terminology for boundary tails or boundary half-nodes
(
r
-tails et cetera), or conflate the terminology.

As in Definition
1.29
(2), we write for a pre-graded open
{(r,s)}
-spin surface:

•

k_{1}=k_{\{1\}}
, the number of
(r-2,0)
-twisted boundary points,

•

k_{2}=k_{\{2\}}
, the number of
(0,s-2)
-twisted boundary points, and

•

k_{12}=k_{\{1,2\}}
, the number of
(r-2,s-2)
-twisted points.

Observation 2.25
.

It follows from (
1.14
)
that the twists
(a_{i},b_{i})
for a twisted
{(r,s)}
-spin structure on a closed, smooth marked genus
0
orbifold Riemann surface satisfy

(2.21)

e_{1}=\frac{\sum a_{i}-(r-2)}{r}\in\mathbb{Z},~~e_{2}=\frac{\sum b_{i}-(s-2)}{s}\in\mathbb{Z}.

We have that the complex ranks of the two Witten bundle components in the closed case are
e_{1},e_{2}
.

Similarly, Observation
1.35
gives the following:

Observation 2.26
.

The internal twists
(a_{i},b_{i})
for a pre-graded
{(r,s)}
-spin structure on a smooth genus
0
marked orbifold Riemann surface with boundary satisfy:

(2.22)

e_{1}=\frac{2\sum a_{i}+(k_{1}+k_{12}-1)(r-2)}{r}\in\mathbb{Z},~~e_{2}=\frac{2\sum b_{i}+(k_{2}+k_{12}-1)(s-2)}{s}\in\mathbb{Z},

and

(2.23)

e_{1}\equiv k_{1}+k_{12}-1~(\text{mod}~2),~~e_{2}\equiv k_{2}+k_{12}-1~(\text{mod}~2).

Here,
e_{1},e_{2}
are the ranks of the real Witten bundles, see (
2.10
).

For the benefit of the reader we now specialize Notation
1.45
and Definition
2.2
to the rank
2
case.

Notation 2.27
.

For a finite set
I\subseteq\mathbb{N}\subseteq\Omega
,
write
\Gamma=\Gamma^{W}_{0,k_{1},k_{2},k_{12},\{(a_{i},b_{i})\}_{i\in I}}
for the graded graph with

•

one vertex
v
;

•

|I|
internal tails marked bijectively to
I
, with the
i
th
tail having twist
\operatorname{tw}=(a_{i},b_{i})
;

•

k_{1}
boundary
r
-tails and
k_{2}
boundary
s
-tails marked by
\emptyset
(that is, unlabeled);

•

k_{12}
boundary tails with
\text{tw}=(r-2,s-2)
and
{\text{alt}}=(1,1)
marked by
\emptyset
;

•

\Pi=(\Pi_{v})
consisting of all possible cyclic orders for boundary tails.

We define
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{0,k_{1},k_{2},k_{12},\{(a_{i},b_{i})\}_{i\in I}}^{W}:={\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{\Gamma}^{W},
and
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{0,k_{1},k_{2},k_{12},\{(a_{i},b_{i})\}_{i\in I}}^{W,\text{labeled}}
for its labeled version.

Remark 2.28
.

There is an important difference between the conventions for our moduli and those of
[
42
,
5
,
6
]
. In those references, boundary markings are labeled; however, we do not label them. This corresponds to taking some quotients in the moduli defined in those papers, see Step (F) of the proof of
Proposition
2.4
.

In the case where
k_{12}=1
, the labelled moduli space
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{0,k_{1},k_{2},k_{12},\{(a_{i},b_{i})\}_{i\in I}}^{W,\text{labeled}}
has
(k_{1}+k_{2})!
connected components. This can be understood by computing the number of cyclic orders of
k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{12}
boundary markings. However, in the unlabeled case, there are additional automorphisms of the disks which permute the boundary markings that preserve the twists. Without loss of generality, we can view any cyclic ordering as an ordering that starts at the root. There are
\binom{k_{1}+k_{2}}{k_{1}}
orderings starting with the root that preserve the relative ordering of
r
-points and
s
-points. Each of these orderings correspond to a connected component of the moduli
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{0,k_{1},k_{2},k_{12},\{(a_{i},b_{i})\}_{i\in I}}^{W}
.

Example 2.29
.

Consider Example
2.7
where we have one internal marked point and three boundary marked points. Suppose further that the boundary marked points consist of one root, one
r
-point, and one
s
-point.
Here, the moduli
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{0,1,1,1,\{(1,1)\}}^{W}
has two connected components, depending on the cyclic orderings of the three boundary markings.

On the other hand, if we suppose that the boundary marked points consist of one root and two
r
-points, then the moduli
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{0,2,0,1,\{(2,0)\}}^{W}
has only one connected component. Indeed, the two
r
-points will become indistinguishable and there will exist an isomorphism for the
W
-spin disks that will identify the two hexagons. This can be viewed as quotienting the labeled moduli space
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{0,2,0,1,\{(2,0)\}}^{W,\text{labeled}}
(consisting of two connected components) by this extra involution, so the moduli
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{0,2,0,1,\{(2,0)\}}^{W}
is the hexagon in Figure
8
.

Notation 2.30
.

Let
I
index a set of internal markings with twists
(a_{i},b_{i})
and
\mathbf{d}=(d_{i})_{i\in I}
be a vector of descendents at each internal marking. Write
r(I)\in\{0,\dots,r-1\}
and
s(I)\in\{0,\dots,s-1\}
for the unique numbers so that

(2.24)

\displaystyle\begin{split}r(I)\equiv{}&\sum_{j\in I}a_{j}~(\text{mod}~r),\\
s(I)\equiv{}&\sum_{j\in I}b_{j}~(\text{mod}~s),\text{ and }\\
m(I,\mathbf{d}):={}&rs+\sum_{j\in I}\left(sa_{j}+rb_{j}+rs(d_{j}-1)\right).\end{split}

If
J\subseteq I
, we write
m(J,\mathbf{d}):=m(J,\mathbf{d}|_{J})
.

Proposition 2.31
.

Consider a smooth rooted connected
{(r,s)}
-graph
\Gamma:=\Gamma_{0,k_{1},k_{2},1,\{(a_{i},b_{i})\}_{i\in I}}
with a set of internal tails
I
. Then we have the following:

(a)

k_{1}\equiv r(I)~(\text{mod}~r)
and
k_{2}\equiv s(I)~(\text{mod}~s)
if and only if the space
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}^{W}_{\Gamma}
is nonempty.

(b)

Assume that the space
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}^{W}_{\Gamma}
is nonempty. The equation
sk_{1}+rk_{2}=m(I,\mathbf{d})
holds if and only if

(2.25)

\operatorname{rank}(E_{\Gamma}(\mathbf{d}))=\dim{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}^{W}_{\Gamma}.

Proof.

(a) The backwards implication is immediate from (
2.22
) and (
2.23
). We know, since
k_{12}(\Gamma)=1
, that

e_{1}=k_{1}+\frac{2(\sum a_{i}-k_{1})}{r}\in\mathbb{Z},\qquad e_{1}\equiv k_{1}\pmod{2}.

This implies that
\frac{2(\sum a_{i}-k_{1})}{r}\in 2\mathbb{Z}
, which requires that
r
divides
(\sum a_{i}-k_{1})
. The analogue for the
s(I)
follows similarly. The forwards implication follows from Proposition
1.26
.

(b) We start by noting that Equation (
2.25
) is equivalent to the equation

(2.26)

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in I}2d_{i}+\frac{2\sum_{i\in I(\Gamma)}a_{i}+(k_{1}(\Gamma)+k_{12}(\Gamma)-1)(r-2)}{r}

\displaystyle+\frac{2\sum_{i\in I(\Gamma)}b_{i}+(k_{2}(\Gamma)+k_{12}(\Gamma)-1)(s-2)}{s}

\displaystyle=2|I|+k_{1}(\Gamma)+k_{2}(\Gamma)+k_{12}(\Gamma)-3.

By multiplying by
rs
, using that
k_{12}(\Gamma)=1
, and subtracting
rs(k_{1}(\Gamma)+k_{2}(\Gamma))
from both sides, we have that the above equation is equivalent to:

(2.27)

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in I}2rsd_{i}+2\sum_{i\in I}sa_{i}-2sk_{1}(\Gamma)+2\sum_{i\in I}rb_{i}-2rk_{2}(\Gamma)

\displaystyle=2rs|I|-2rs.

We then can divide by two and isolate
sk_{1}(\Gamma)+rk_{2}(\Gamma)
on one side of the equation to see:

(2.28)

\displaystyle sk_{1}(\Gamma)+rk_{2}(\Gamma)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i\in I}rsd_{i}+\sum_{i\in I}sa_{i}+\sum_{i\in I}rb_{i}-rs|I|+rs

\displaystyle=rs+\sum_{i\in I}(rs(d_{i}-1)+sa_{i}+rb_{i})

\displaystyle=m(I,\mathbf{d}).

∎

Proposition
2.31
(b) provides a combinatorial description on the level of graphs for when (
2.25
) holds, and we will consequently have non-trivial enumerative invariants. We give the following definition for such graphs:

Definition 2.32
.

Let
I
be a set of markings with twists
(a_{i},b_{i})
,
i\in I
,
and
\mathbf{d}=(d_{i})_{i\in I}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{I}
a vector of descendents.
A smooth rooted connected graded
{(r,s)}
-graph
\Gamma
with
I(\Gamma)\subseteq I
is
balanced
for
\mathbf{d}
if
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}^{W}_{\Gamma}
is nonempty and

\operatorname{rank}E_{\Gamma}(\mathbf{d})=\dim{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}^{W}_{\Gamma}.

For
J\subseteq I
, denote by
{\text{Balanced}}(J,\mathbf{d})
the collection of connected rooted graphs with internal markings
J
that are balanced for
\mathbf{d}
. If instead
\Gamma
is a disconnected smooth rooted

{(r,s)}
-graph, it is balanced for
\mathbf{d}
if all its connected components are balanced for
\mathbf{d}
.

We also can define critical graphs, which will correspond to irreducible components of a codimension one boundary strata of
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}^{W}_{0,k_{1},k_{2},1,\{(a_{i},b_{i})\}}
that yield wall-crossing (see Theorem
5.9

and §
5.3
). Recall the definition of a relevant graph given in Definition
1.57
.

Definition 2.33
.

Let
I
be a set of internal markings and
\mathbf{d}=(d_{i})_{i\in I}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{I}
.

(1)

A smooth connected relevant graded
{(r,s)}
-graph
\Gamma
with
I(\Gamma)\subseteq I
and without fully twisted boundary tails is
critical
for
\mathbf{d}
if

(2.29)

\operatorname{rank}(E_{\Gamma}(\mathbf{d}))=\dim{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}^{W}_{\Gamma}+1.

For
J\subseteq I
, we denote by
{\text{Crit}}(J,\mathbf{d})
the collection of critical graphs for
\mathbf{d}
with internal markings
J
.

(2)

A (connected) graded graph
\Lambda\in\partial\Gamma
, for
\Gamma\in{\text{Balanced}}(J,\mathbf{d})
, is a
critical boundary graph
if it has a unique edge which is a boundary edge and after detaching this edge the graph is separated into two vertices
v_{0},v_{1}
such that
v_{0}\in{\text{Crit}}(I(v_{0}),\mathbf{d})
and
v_{1}\in{\text{Balanced}}(I(v_{1}),\mathbf{d})
.

(3)

A graded graph
\Lambda\in\partial\Gamma
where
\Gamma
is
balanced is a
critical boundary graph
if one connected component
is a critical boundary graph and the remaining components are balanced.

(4)

If
\Gamma
is balanced, a graph
\Lambda\in\partial\Gamma

is an
exchangeable critical boundary
if

\Lambda\in\partial^{\textup{exchange}}\Gamma
and
\Lambda
is a critical boundary graph.

Notation 2.34
.

When
\mathbf{d}
is the zero vector we do not add the phrase “for
\mathbf{d}
” and omit
\mathbf{d}
from the notations of
Crit
and
Balanced
.
We write

{\text{Crit}}(\subsetneq I,\mathbf{d})=\bigcup_{J\subsetneq I}{\text{Crit}}(J,\mathbf{d}),\quad{\text{Crit}}(\subseteq I,\mathbf{d})={\text{Crit}}(\subsetneq I,\mathbf{d})\cup{\text{Crit}}(I,\mathbf{d}),

and similarly for
{\text{Balanced}}(\subsetneq I,\mathbf{d})
and
{\text{Balanced}}(\subseteq I,\mathbf{d})
.

Definition 2.35
.

A stratum
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{\Lambda}
in the moduli space
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{\Gamma}
is relevant, critical etc. if the corresponding graph
\Lambda\in\partial\Gamma
is.

Definition 2.36
.

With the same notation as in Definition
2.33
(2), a
top boundary
for
\Gamma\in{\text{Balanced}}(I,\mathbf{d})
is a critical boundary of
\Gamma
such that
I(v_{1})=\emptyset
.

.

\displaystyle+\frac{2\sum_{i\in I(\Gamma)}b_{i}+(k_{2}(\Gamma)-1)(s-2)}{s}

\displaystyle=2|I|+k_{1}(\Gamma)+k_{2}(\Gamma)-2.
