\displaystyle=\frac{k_{1}(0)+1}{{k_{1}(0)+k_{1}(j)}}\#Z(H^{\Xi}(t^{\Xi}_{r}))+\frac{k_{1}(j)-1}{{k_{1}(0)+k_{1}(j)}}\#Z(H^{\Xi}(t^{\Xi}_{s}))

\displaystyle\alpha^{\Xi}_{s,1}

\displaystyle=\#Z(H^{\Xi}(t^{\Xi}_{s}))

\displaystyle\alpha^{\Xi}_{{\times},1}

\displaystyle=\#Z(H^{\Xi}(t^{\Xi}_{s}))

\displaystyle\alpha^{\Xi}_{z_{2},1}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\#Z(H^{\Xi}(t^{\Xi}_{z_{2}}))&\text{ if $2\in I_{0}$}\\
\#Z(H^{\Xi}(t^{\Xi}_{s}))&\text{ if $2\in I_{j}$}\end{cases}

\displaystyle\alpha^{\Xi}_{r,2}

\displaystyle=\#Z(H^{\Xi}(t^{\Xi}_{r}))

\displaystyle\alpha^{\Xi}_{s,2}

\displaystyle=\frac{k_{2}(j)-1}{{k_{2}(0)+k_{2}(j)}}\#Z(H^{\Xi}(t^{\Xi}_{r}))+\frac{k_{2}(0)+1}{{k_{2}(0)+k_{2}(j)}}\#Z(H^{\Xi}(t^{\Xi}_{s}))

\displaystyle\alpha^{\Xi}_{{\times},2}

\displaystyle=\#Z(H^{\Xi}(t^{\Xi}_{r}))

\displaystyle\alpha^{\Xi}_{z_{2},2}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\#Z(H^{\Xi}(t^{\Xi}_{z_{2}}))&\text{ if $2\in I_{0}$}\\
\#Z(H^{\Xi}(t^{\Xi}_{r}))&\text{ if $2\in I_{j}$}\end{cases}

Proof of Lemma
6.13
.

The proof is a direct computation using the definition of
H^{\Gamma_{P}}(t_{\bullet})
.
We start by recalling that by using (
6.23
) we have that

(6.31)

\displaystyle Z^{\Gamma}_{WC}(\mathbf{t}_{w})

\displaystyle:=\sum_{\Lambda\in{\mathcal{G}}_{WC}(P)}\#Z(H(t_{w})|_{[0,1]\times{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Lambda}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}Q\in\mathcal{Q}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1})\\
Q=\{(I_{j},k_{1}(j),k_{2}(j))\}\\
\text{where $1\in I_{0}$ and}\\
P=\widehat{Q}_{j}\text{ for some $j$}\end{subarray}}\left(\sum_{R_{0}=0}^{k_{1}(j)-1}\sum_{\Xi:=\Gamma_{P,j,1,I_{0},k_{1}(0),k_{2}(0),R_{0},S_{0}}\in A^{1}_{Q,j,R_{0}}}\#Z(H(t_{w})|_{[0,1]\times{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Xi}})\right.

\displaystyle+\left.\sum_{S_{0}=1}^{k_{2}(j)-1}\sum_{\Xi:=\Gamma_{P,j,2,I_{0},k_{1}(0),k_{2}(0),R_{0},S_{0}}\in A^{2}_{Q,j,S_{0}}}\#Z(H(t_{w})|_{[0,1]\times{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Xi}})\right).

Recall that for any
\Gamma_{P,j,1,I_{0},k_{1}(0),k_{2}(0),R_{0},S_{0}}\in A^{1}_{Q,j,R_{0}}
or
\Gamma_{P,j,2,I_{0},k_{1}(0),k_{2}(0),R_{0},S_{0}}\in A^{2}_{Q,j,S_{0}}
, we have that

{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{P,j,\epsilon,I_{0},k_{1}(0),k_{2}(0),R_{0},S_{0}}\prec\Lambda_{Q},

where
\epsilon\in\{1,2\}
and
\Lambda_{Q}
is the graph given in Notation
5.7
.

Let
\Lambda_{\epsilon}:=\Gamma_{P,j,\epsilon,I_{0},k_{1}(0),k_{2}(0),R_{0},S_{0}}
. While
\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2},
and
\rho
can all be pulled back from the base,
t_{w}
may not be and hence the homotopy
H^{\Gamma_{P}}(t_{\bullet})|_{\Lambda_{i}}
given in (
6.9
) may not be pulled back from the base in the summations in (
6.31
).
However, we can use Lemma
6.12
to see that the quantities in (
6.31
) are equal to those that can be pulled back from the base as follows:

(6.32)

\displaystyle\#Z(H(t_{r})|_{[0,1]\times{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Lambda_{1}}})

\displaystyle=\frac{k_{1}(0)+1}{k_{1}(0)+k_{1}(j)}\#Z(H(t^{v_{0}}_{r})|_{[0,1]\times{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Lambda_{1}}})

\displaystyle+\frac{k_{1}(j)-1}{k_{1}(0)+k_{1}(j)}\#Z(H(t^{v_{0}}_{s})|_{[0,1]\times{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Lambda_{1}}});

\displaystyle\#Z(H(t_{r})|_{[0,1]\times{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Lambda_{2}}})

\displaystyle=\#Z(H(t^{v_{0}}_{r})|_{[0,1]\times{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Lambda_{2}}});

\displaystyle\#Z(H(t_{s})|_{[0,1]\times{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Lambda_{1}}})

\displaystyle=\#Z(H(t^{v_{0}}_{s})|_{[0,1]\times{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Lambda_{1}}});

\displaystyle\#Z(H(t_{s})|_{[0,1]\times{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Lambda_{2}}})

\displaystyle=\frac{k_{2}(j)-1}{{k_{2}(0)+k_{2}(j)}}\#Z(H(t^{v_{0}}_{r})|_{[0,1]\times{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Lambda_{2}}})

\displaystyle+\frac{k_{2}(0)+1}{{k_{2}(0)+k_{2}(j)}}\#Z(H(t^{v_{0}}_{s})|_{[0,1]\times{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Lambda_{2}}});

\displaystyle\#Z(H(t_{\times})|_{[0,1]\times{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Lambda_{1}}})

\displaystyle=\#Z(H(t^{v_{0}}_{s})|_{[0,1]\times{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Lambda_{1}}});

\displaystyle\#Z(H(t_{\times})|_{[0,1]\times{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Lambda_{2}}})

\displaystyle=\#Z(H(t^{v_{0}}_{r})|_{[0,1]\times{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Lambda_{2}}});

\displaystyle\#Z(H(t_{z_{2}})|_{[0,1]\times{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Lambda_{1}}})

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\#Z(H^{\Xi}(t^{\Xi}_{z_{2}})|_{[0,1]\times{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Lambda_{1}}})&\text{ if $2\in I_{0}$},\\
\#Z(H(t^{v_{0}}_{s})|_{[0,1]\times{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Lambda_{1}}})&\text{ if $2\in I_{j}$};\end{cases}

\displaystyle\#Z(H(t_{z_{2}})|_{[0,1]\times{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Lambda_{2}}})

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\#Z(H^{\Xi}(t^{\Xi}_{z_{2}})|_{[0,1]\times{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Lambda_{2}}})&\text{ if $2\in I_{0}$},\\
\#Z(H(t^{v_{0}}_{r})|_{[0,1]\times{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Lambda_{2}}})&\text{ if $2\in I_{j}$}.\end{cases}

Thus we have reduced the computation of (
6.31
) to the homotopies
H(t^{v_{0}}_{r}),H(t^{v_{0}}_{s}),
and
H(t^{v_{0}}_{z_{2}})
, which all can be pulled back from the base.
Take
H
to be any of these three homotopies. Recall that the homotopy
H
is constant in time on the connected components which do not contain the internal tail
1
, that is, the graphs
\Lambda_{Q,\ell}:=\Gamma_{0,k_{1}(\ell),k_{2}(\ell),1,(a_{i},b_{i})_{i\in I_{\ell}}}
for
1\leq\ell\leq h
. We have the decomposition

[0,1]\times{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Lambda_{Q}}=\prod_{i=1}^{h}{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Lambda_{Q,i}}\times([0,1]\times{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Lambda_{Q,0}}).

In turn, we may decompose the homotopy
H=\left(\boxplus_{i=1}^{h}H_{i}\right)\boxplus H^{v_{0}}
with
H_{i}={\bf s}^{\Lambda_{Q,i}}
and

H^{v_{0}}(t_{\bullet})(u,-)=F_{v_{0}}^{*}(\mathbf{s}_{1}^{v_{0}})\boxplus\left((1-u)t^{v_{0}}_{\bullet}+u(1-u)\rho^{v_{0}}+u\mathbf{s}_{2}^{v_{0}}\right).

By an analogous argument to that used to obtain Equation (
5.22
) and using (
5.22
), we have that

(6.33)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sum_{\Gamma_{P,j,1,I_{0},k_{1}(0),k_{2}(0),R_{0},S_{0}}\in A^{1}_{Q,j,R_{0}}}&\#Z(H|_{[0,1]\times{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Gamma_{P,j,1,I_{0},k_{1}(0),k_{2}(0),R_{0},S_{0}}}})\\
=&(-1)^{k_{2}(0)}(k_{2}(0)+1)\cdot\#Z(H^{v_{0}})\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{h}\#Z({\bf s}^{\Lambda_{Q,j}});\\
\sum_{\Gamma_{P,j,2,I_{0},k_{1}(0),k_{2}(0),R_{0},S_{0}}\in A^{2}_{Q,j,S_{0}}}&\#Z(H|_{[0,1]\times{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Gamma_{P,j,2,I_{0},k_{1}(0),k_{2}(0),R_{0},S_{0}}}})\\
=&(-1)^{k_{2}(0)+1}(k_{1}(0)+1)\cdot\#Z(H^{v_{0}})\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{h}\#Z({\bf s}^{\Lambda_{Q,j}}).\end{split}

Note that

(6.34)

\prod_{i=1}^{h}\#Z({\bf s}^{\Lambda_{Q,j}})=\prod_{i=1}^{h}\left\langle\prod_{\ell\in I_{i}}\tau^{(a_{\ell},b_{\ell})}_{d_{\ell}}\sigma_{1}^{k_{1}(i)}\sigma_{2}^{k_{2}(i)}\sigma_{12}\right\rangle^{\mathbf{s}^{\Gamma_{0,k_{1}(i),k_{2}(i),1,I_{i}}},o}

Moreover, the quantities in (
6.33
) do not depend on
R_{0}
or
S_{0}
respectively. Using Observation
6.11
and (
6.32
), we then also have that

(6.35)

\#Z(H^{v_{0}}(t^{v_{0}}_{\bullet}))=\begin{cases}\alpha^{\Gamma_{0,k_{1}(0)+1,k_{2}(0)+1,0,I_{0}}}_{\bullet,1}&\text{ if $\Lambda\in A^{1}_{Q,j,R_{0}}$}\\
\alpha^{\Gamma_{0,k_{1}(0)+1,k_{2}(0)+1,0,I_{0}}}_{\bullet,2}&\text{ if $\Lambda\in A^{2}_{Q,j,S_{0}}$}\end{cases}

By applying Equations (
6.33
), (
6.34
), and (
6.35
) to simplify (
6.31
), we obtain the claim.
∎

As we can see above, the expression for
\#Z_{WC}^{\Gamma}(\mathbf{t}_{w})
is complicated for an arbitrary
\mathbf{t}_{w}
. We instead find specific families of non-trivial linear combinations of
\#Z_{WC}^{\Gamma}(\mathbf{t}_{w})
for different graphs
\Gamma
and points
w
which vanish.

Notation 6.14
.

Let
Q=\{(I_{0},k_{1}(0)+1,k_{2}(0)+1),\ldots,(I_{h},k_{1}(h),k_{2}(h))\}\in\mathcal{Q}^{\neq 0}_{h}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1})
, defined in Notation
5.7
.
There are
j+2
different elements of
\mathcal{P}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1})
which we can naturally build from
Q
. First, recall from (
5.12
) that

\widehat{Q}_{j}:=\{(I_{1},k_{1}(1),k_{2}(1)),\ldots,(I_{0}\cup I_{j},k_{1}(0)+k_{1}(j),k_{2}(0)+k_{2}(j)),\ldots,(I_{h},k_{1}(h),k_{2}(h))\}\in\mathcal{P}_{h}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1})\\

for all
1\leq j\leq h
. Second, we define the following:

(6.36)

\displaystyle\widehat{Q}^{+r}

\displaystyle:=\{(I_{1},k_{1}(1),k_{2}(1)),\ldots,(I_{h},k_{1}(h),k_{2}(h)),(I_{0},k_{1}(0)+r,k_{2}(0))\}\in\mathcal{P}_{h+1}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1});

\displaystyle\widehat{Q}^{+s}

\displaystyle:=\{(I_{1},k_{1}(1),k_{2}(1)),\ldots,(I_{h},k_{1}(h),k_{2}(h)),(I_{0},k_{1}(0),k_{2}(0)+s)\}\in\mathcal{P}_{h+1}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1}).

Note that
\widehat{Q}^{+r}=\widehat{Q^{+r}}_{h+1}
and
\widehat{Q}^{+s}=\widehat{Q^{+s}}_{h+1}
, where
Q^{+r}
and
Q^{+s}
are as defined in (
5.6
).

6.2.
Special linear combinations of the distinguished multisections

Definition 6.15
.

Let
\{(a^{P},b^{P},c^{P},d^{P})\}_{P\in\mathcal{P}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1})}
be a family of 4-tuples of rational numbers so that the 4-tuples do not depend on the order of the elements of
P
and
a^{P}+b^{P}+c^{P}+d^{P}=1
.

(1)

The family of 4-tuples satisfies
Property
Q1
if the family
further satisfies the following conditions:

(a)

For all
P
,
d^{P}=0
.

(b)

For any
h\geq 0
and
Q=\{(I_{0},k_{1}(0)+1,k_{2}(0)+1),\ldots,(I_{h},k_{1}(h),k_{2}(h))\}\in\mathcal{Q}^{\neq 0}_{h}(I,\mathbf{d})
with
1\in I_{0}
, we have that

(6.37)

\frac{1+\sum_{i=0}^{h}k_{1}(i)}{k_{1}(0)+r}a^{\widehat{Q}^{+r}}+\frac{1+\sum_{i=0}^{h}k_{2}(i)}{k_{2}(0)+s}b^{\widehat{Q}^{+s}}=1+\sum_{j=1}^{h}\left(\frac{k_{1}(j)}{k_{1}(0)+k_{1}(j)}a^{\widehat{Q}_{j}}+\frac{k_{2}(j)}{k_{2}(0)+k_{2}(j)}b^{\widehat{Q}_{j}}\right).

(2)

The family of 4-tuples satisfies
Property
Q2
if the family
further satisfies the following conditions:

(a)

If
P
satisfies
\{1,2\}\subseteq I_{j}
for some
j
then

d^{P}=1
and
a^{P}=b^{P}=c^{P}=0
. Otherwise
d^{P}=0
.

(b)

For any
h\geq 0
and
Q=\{(I_{0},k_{1}(0)+1,k_{2}(0)+1),\ldots,(I_{h},k_{1}(h),k_{2}(h))\}\in\mathcal{Q}^{\neq 0}_{h}(I,\mathbf{d})
where
1\in I_{0}
and
2\notin I_{0}
, we have that

(6.38)

\frac{1+\sum_{i=0}^{h}k_{1}(i)}{k_{1}(0)+r}a^{\widehat{Q}^{+r}}+\frac{1+\sum_{i=0}^{h}k_{2}(i)}{k_{2}(0)+s}b^{\widehat{Q}^{+s}}=1+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j=1\\
2\notin I_{j}\end{subarray}}^{h}\left(\frac{k_{1}(j)}{k_{1}(0)+k_{1}(j)}a^{\widehat{Q}_{j}}+\frac{k_{2}(j)}{k_{2}(0)+k_{2}(j)}b^{\widehat{Q}_{j}}\right).

Remark 6.16
.

Note that, in the case
h=0
in Definition
6.15
and in the proofs below, we remark that we will often make use of the summation from
j=1
to
h
being empty and equal to zero.

Lemma 6.17
.

There exists two families of 4-tuples
\{(a^{P},b^{P},c^{P},d^{P})\}_{P\in\mathcal{P}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1})}
of rational numbers, one of which satisfies Property
Q1
and another which satisfies Property
Q2
.

Proof.

We first prove the case of Property
Q1
inductively on
h
.
The inductive hypothesis is: there are choices of
a^{P},b^{P}
for all

P\in\mathcal{P}_{\leq h+1}(I,\mathbf{d})
, with
a^{P},b^{P}
not depending
on the order of elements
P
, such that (
6.37
) holds
for all
Q\in{\mathcal{Q}}^{\not=\emptyset}_{\leq h}(I,\mathbf{d})
.

The case where
h=-1
is vacuously true. Now suppose the inductive hypothesis is true for
h
. In doing so, we have determined the constants
a^{P},b^{P}
for all
P\in\mathcal{P}_{\leq h+1}(I,\mathbf{d})
. Given
Q\in\mathcal{Q}^{\not=\emptyset}_{h+1}(I,\mathbf{d})
, the right-hand side of its corresponding equation (
6.37
) is then determined as
\widehat{Q}_{j}\in\mathcal{P}_{h+1}(I,\mathbf{d})
for all
j
. Now note that, given
P\in\mathcal{P}_{h+2}(I,\mathbf{d})
, there is at most one
Q\in\mathcal{Q}^{\not=\emptyset}_{h+1}(I,\mathbf{d})
so that
a^{P}
is in the equation (
6.37
), namely
P=\widehat{Q}^{+r}
. Similarly,
b^{P}
appears in (
6.37
) for some
Q\in\mathcal{Q}^{\not=\emptyset}_{h+1}(I,\mathbf{d})
only if
P=\widehat{Q}^{+s}
. Thus each variable
a^{P},b^{P}
appears in at most one equation in the system
(
6.37
) (where we run over all
Q
).
This determines the values of
a^{P},b^{P}
for those elements of

\mathcal{P}_{h+2}(I,\mathbf{d})
of the form
\widehat{Q}^{+r}
or

\widehat{Q}^{+s}
. We remark here that if we permute the order of the tuples in
Q
, the summation on the right-hand side of (
6.37
) will remain invariant by the induction hypothesis, hence the values of
a^{P}
and
b^{P}
for
P=\widehat{Q}^{+r},\widehat{Q}^{+s}
can be chosen to not depend on the order of its tuples. We may then choose arbitrary values for all other
a^{P},b^{P}

for
P\in\mathcal{P}_{h+2}(I,\mathbf{d})
subject to the constraint of
being independent of ordering. Further, we define
c^{P}=1-a^{P}-b^{P}
.
Thus there exists such a family that has Property
Q1
.

An analogous inductive argument using (
6.38
) yields a family
satisfying Property
Q2
.
∎

We now have the following lemma:

Lemma 6.18
.

Let
\{(a^{P},b^{P},c^{P},d^{P})\}_{P\in\mathcal{P}(I,\mathbf{d})}
be a family of 4-tuples of rational numbers that satisfies either property
Q1
or
Q2
. Then

(6.39)

\displaystyle\sum_{h=1}^{|I|}\frac{1}{h!}

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}P\in\mathcal{P}_{h}(I,\mathbf{d}),\\
P=\{(I_{1},k_{1}(1),k_{2}(1)),\ldots,(I_{h},k_{1}(h),k_{2}(h))\}\end{subarray}}\frac{\Gamma(\frac{1+\sum_{i=1}^{h}k_{1}(i)}{r})\Gamma(\frac{1+\sum_{i=1}^{h}k_{2}(i)}{s})}{\Gamma(\frac{1+r(I)}{r})\Gamma(\frac{1+s(I)}{s})}\cdot

\displaystyle\quad\quad\cdot\left(a^{P}Z_{WC}^{\Gamma_{P}}(\mathbf{t}_{r})+b^{P}Z_{WC}^{\Gamma_{P}}(\mathbf{t}_{s})+c^{P}Z_{WC}^{\Gamma_{P}}(\mathbf{t}_{\times})+d^{P}Z^{\Gamma_{P}}_{WC}(\mathbf{t}_{z_{2}})\right)=0.

Proof.

First, we recall the collection
{\widetilde{\text{Crit}}}(P)

of (
5.9
).
Second, we recall the collection
\overline{\mathcal{Q}}(I,\mathbf{d})

defined in Part (E) of the proof of Theorem
5.4
of ordered sets

Q=\{(I_{0},k_{1}(0)+1,k_{2}(0)+1),\ldots,(I_{h},k_{1}(h),k_{2}(h))\}\in\mathcal{Q}_{h}(I,\mathbf{d}),\ h\geq 0

so that

•

I_{j}\neq\varnothing
for all
0\leq j\leq h
;

•

the elements
(I_{1},k_{1}(1),k_{2}(1)),\ldots,(I_{h},k_{1}(h),k_{2}(h))
are ordered in a way so that, for any two indices
i
and
j
, we have that
\text{min}(I_{i})<\text{min}(I_{j})
if and only if
i<j
.

As seen in (
6.12
), the quantity
Z^{\Gamma_{P}}_{WC}(H^{\Gamma_{P}}(t_{\bullet}))
only sums over
\mathcal{G}_{WC}(P)
, which are those graphs
\Lambda\in{\widetilde{\text{Crit}}}(P)
where
1\in I_{0}
. If
Q\in\overline{\mathcal{Q}}(I,\mathbf{d})\cap{\mathcal{Q}}_{0}(I,\mathbf{d})
,
then
\Lambda_{Q}
is not the base of any critical boundary graph.
If
h>0
then
Q\in\mathcal{Q}^{\neq\emptyset}(I,\mathbf{d})
so
\widehat{Q}_{j}
is defined for all
1\leq j\leq h
.

Given
Q\in\overline{\mathcal{Q}}(I,\mathbf{d}),\bullet\in\{{\times},r,s,z_{2}\}
, we will write

O_{i}(Q):=\left\langle\prod_{\ell\in I_{i}}\tau^{(a_{\ell},b_{\ell})}_{d_{\ell}}\sigma_{1}^{k_{1}(i)}\sigma_{2}^{k_{2}(i)}\sigma_{12}\right\rangle^{\mathbf{s}^{\Gamma_{0,k_{1}(i),k_{2}(i),1,I_{i}}},o}

and

X_{Q,\bullet}:=\#Z(H^{\Gamma_{0,k_{1}(0)+1,k_{2}(0)+1,0,I_{0}}}(t^{\Gamma_{0,k_{1}(0)+1,k_{2}(0)+1,0,I_{0}}}_{\bullet}))\prod_{i=1}^{h}O_{i}(Q).

We note that the quantity
X_{Q,\bullet}
is invariant with respect to permuting the last
h
triplets of
Q
.

For any
Q\in\overline{\mathcal{Q}}_{h}(I,\mathbf{d})
, we
recall the definition of
Q^{+r}
and
Q^{+s}
from (
5.6
).
We define
\overline{\mathcal{Q}}^{r}_{h+1}(I,\mathbf{d})
and
\overline{\mathcal{Q}}^{s}_{h+1}(I,\mathbf{d})
to be the collections of tuples that can be written in the form
Q^{+r}
and
Q^{+s}

for
Q\in\overline{\mathcal{Q}}_{h}(I,\mathbf{d})
. Take

\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{h}(I,\mathbf{d}):=\overline{\mathcal{Q}}_{h}(I,\mathbf{d})\cup\overline{\mathcal{Q}}^{r}_{h}(I,\mathbf{d})\cup\overline{\mathcal{Q}}^{s}_{h}(I,\mathbf{d}).

Define
\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}(I,\mathbf{d}):=\bigcup_{h}\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{h}(I,\mathbf{d})
.
As in (
5.23
), we have that

(6.40)

X_{Q,\bullet}=-X_{Q^{+r},\bullet}=-X_{Q^{+s},\bullet}.

We consider the quantities
X_{Q,\bullet}
for all
Q\in\overline{\mathcal{Q}}(I,\mathbf{d})
and
\bullet\in\{{\times},r,s,z_{2}\}
as independent linear variables. By Lemma
6.13
and Equation (
6.40
), we can write any of
Z^{\Gamma}_{WC}(H^{\Gamma}(t_{r}))
,
Z^{\Gamma}_{WC}(H^{\Gamma}(t_{s}))
,
Z^{\Gamma}_{WC}(H^{\Gamma}(t_{\times}))
, or
Z^{\Gamma}_{WC}(H^{\Gamma}(t_{z_{2}}))
, as a linear combination of the variables
X_{Q,\bullet}
. Thus, we can write the left hand side of Equation (
6.39
) as a linear combination of the variables
X_{Q,\bullet}
.

We now compute the coefficient of
X_{Q,\bullet}
in Equation (
6.39
) for all
Q\in\overline{\mathcal{Q}}(I,\mathbf{d})
and
\bullet\in\{{\times},r,s,z_{2}\}
.
Using Lemma
6.13
, we can see that the left hand side of (
6.39
) can be expanded to be:

(6.41)

\displaystyle\sum_{h=1}^{|I|}

\displaystyle\sum_{P\in\mathcal{P}_{h}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1})}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{h}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1})\\
Q=\{(I_{0},k_{1}(0),k_{2}(0)),\ldots,(I_{h},k_{1}(h),k_{2}(h))\}\\
\text{where $1\in I_{0}$ and}\\
P=\widehat{Q}_{j}\text{ for some $j$}\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{h!}\frac{\Gamma(\frac{1+\sum_{i=0}^{h}k_{1}(i)}{r})\Gamma(\frac{1+\sum_{i=0}^{h}k_{2}(i)}{s})}{\Gamma(\frac{1+r(I)}{r})\Gamma(\frac{1+s(I)}{s})}\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{h}O_{i}(Q)\cdot

\displaystyle(-1)^{k_{2}(0)}\left(a^{P}\Big((k_{2}(0)+1)k_{1}(j)\alpha^{\Gamma_{0,k_{1}(0)+1,k_{2}(0)+1,0,I_{0}}}_{r,1}-(k_{1}(0)+1)k_{2}(j)\alpha^{\Gamma_{0,k_{1}(0)+1,k_{2}(0)+1,0,I_{0}}}_{r,2}\Big)\right.

\displaystyle+b^{P}\Big((k_{2}(0)+1)k_{1}(j)\alpha^{\Gamma_{0,k_{1}(0)+1,k_{2}(0)+1,0,I_{0}}}_{s,1}-(k_{1}(0)+1)k_{2}(j)\alpha^{\Gamma_{0,k_{1}(0)+1,k_{2}(0)+1,0,I_{0}}}_{s,2}\Big)

\displaystyle+c^{P}\Big((k_{2}(0)+1)k_{1}(j)\alpha^{\Gamma_{0,k_{1}(0)+1,k_{2}(0)+1,0,I_{0}}}_{{\times},1}-(k_{1}(0)+1)k_{2}(j)\alpha^{\Gamma_{0,k_{1}(0)+1,k_{2}(0)+1,0,I_{0}}}_{{\times},2}\Big)

\displaystyle+d^{P}\left.\Big((k_{2}(0)+1)k_{1}(j)\alpha^{\Gamma_{0,k_{1}(0)+1,k_{2}(0)+1,0,I_{0}}}_{z_{2},1}-(k_{1}(0)+1)k_{2}(j)\alpha^{\Gamma_{0,k_{1}(0)+1,k_{2}(0)+1,0,I_{0}}}_{z_{2},2}\Big)\right).

We can change the summations over
\mathcal{P}_{h}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1})
and special subsets of

to a summation over

using the partition given in Equation (

) and the fact that there are

elements of

. We may then rewrite the summation

(6.42)

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}Q\in\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1})\\
Q=\{(I_{0},k_{1}(0),k_{2}(0)),\ldots,(I_{h},k_{1}(h),k_{2}(h))\}\\
\text{where $1\in I_{0}$ }\end{subarray}}WC(Q)

where

is defined as follows.

•

If
Q\in\overline{Q}_{h}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1})

(6.43)

\displaystyle WC(Q):=

\displaystyle(-1)^{k_{2}(0)}\sum_{j=1}^{h}\frac{\Gamma(\frac{1+\sum_{i=0}^{h}k_{1}(i)}{r})\Gamma(\frac{1+\sum_{i=0}^{h}k_{2}(i)}{s})}{\Gamma(\frac{1+r(I)}{r})\Gamma(\frac{1+s(I)}{s})}\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{h}O_{i}(Q)\cdot

\displaystyle\left((k_{2}(0)+1)k_{1}(j)\left(a^{\widehat{Q}_{j}}\alpha^{\Gamma_{0,k_{1}(0)+1,k_{2}(0)+1,0,I_{0}}}_{r,1}+b^{\widehat{Q}_{j}}\alpha^{\Gamma_{0,k_{1}(0)+1,k_{2}(0)+1,0,I_{0}}}_{s,1}+\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.c^{\widehat{Q}_{j}}\alpha^{\Gamma_{0,k_{1}(0)+1,k_{2}(0)+1,0,I_{0}}}_{{\times},1}+d^{\widehat{Q}_{j}}\alpha^{\Gamma_{0,k_{1}(0)+1,k_{2}(0)+1,0,I_{0}}}_{z_{2},1}\right)\right.

\displaystyle-(k_{1}(0)+1)k_{2}(j)\left(a^{\widehat{Q}_{j}}\alpha^{\Gamma_{0,k_{1}(0)+1,k_{2}(0)+1,0,I_{0}}}_{r,2}+b^{\widehat{Q}_{j}}\alpha^{\Gamma_{0,k_{1}(0)+1,k_{2}(0)+1,0,I_{0}}}_{s,2}+\right.

If

,

\displaystyle WC(Q^{+r}):=

\displaystyle-(-1)^{k_{2}(0)}\frac{\Gamma(\frac{1+\sum_{i=0}^{h}k_{1}(i)}{r})\Gamma(\frac{1+\sum_{i=0}^{h}k_{2}(i)}{s})}{\Gamma(\frac{1+r(I)}{r})\Gamma(\frac{1+s(I)}{s})}\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{h-1}O_{i}(Q)\cdot

\displaystyle(k_{2}(0)+1)r\left(a^{\widehat{Q}^{+r}}\alpha^{\Gamma_{0,k_{1}(0)+1,k_{2}(0)+1,0,I_{0}}}_{r,1}\right.+b^{\widehat{Q}^{+r}}\alpha^{\Gamma_{0,k_{1}(0)+1,k_{2}(0)+1,0,I_{0}}}_{s,1}

\displaystyle+c^{\widehat{Q}^{+r}}\alpha^{\Gamma_{0,k_{1}(0)+1,k_{2}(0)+1,0,I_{0}}}_{{\times},1}+d^{\widehat{Q}^{+r}}\left.\alpha^{\Gamma_{0,k_{1}(0)+1,k_{2}(0)+1,0,I_{0}}}_{z_{2},1}\right).

If

, then we can use the facts that

\displaystyle WC(Q^{+s}):=

\displaystyle(-1)^{k_{2}(0)}\frac{\Gamma(\frac{1+\sum_{i=0}^{h}k_{1}(i)}{r})\Gamma(\frac{1+\sum_{i=0}^{h}k_{2}(i)}{s})}{\Gamma(\frac{1+r(I)}{r})\Gamma(\frac{1+s(I)}{s})}\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{h-1}O_{i}(Q)\cdot

\displaystyle(k_{1}(0)+1)s\left(a^{\widehat{Q}^{+s}}\alpha^{\Gamma_{0,k_{1}(0)+1,k_{2}(0)+1,0,I_{0}}}_{r,2}\right.+b^{\widehat{Q}^{+s}}\alpha^{\Gamma_{0,k_{1}(0)+1,k_{2}(0)+1,0,I_{0}}}_{s,2}

\displaystyle+c^{\widehat{Q}^{+s}}\alpha^{\Gamma_{0,k_{1}(0)+1,k_{2}(0)+1,0,I_{0}}}_{{\times},2}+d^{\widehat{Q}^{+s}}\left.\alpha^{\Gamma_{0,k_{1}(0)+1,k_{2}(0)+1,0,I_{0}}}_{z_{2},2}\right).

Using the definition of

and
\alpha^{\Gamma_{0,k_{1}(0)+1,k_{2}(0)+1,0,I_{0}}}_{\bullet,\epsilon}
given in Lemma

, we can see that

(6.46)

\displaystyle WC(Q)=

\displaystyle(-1)^{k_{2}(0)}\sum_{j=1}^{h}\frac{\Gamma(\frac{1+\sum_{i=0}^{h}k_{1}(i)}{r})\Gamma(\frac{1+\sum_{i=0}^{h}k_{2}(i)}{s})}{\Gamma(\frac{1+r(I)}{r})\Gamma(\frac{1+s(I)}{s})}\cdot

\displaystyle\left[(k_{2}(0)+1)k_{1}(j)\cdot\left[a^{\widehat{Q}_{j}}\left(\frac{k_{1}(0)+1}{k_{1}(0)+k_{1}(j)}X_{Q,r}+\frac{k_{1}(j)-1}{k_{1}(0)+k_{1}(j)}X_{Q,s}\right)+b^{\widehat{Q}_{j}}X_{Q,s}+\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.c^{\widehat{Q}_{j}}X_{Q,s}+\begin{cases}d^{\widehat{Q}_{j}}X_{Q,z_{2}}&\text{ if $2\in I_{0}$}\\
d^{\widehat{Q}_{j}}X_{Q,s}&\text{ if $2\in I_{j}$}\\
0&\text{ otherwise}\end{cases}\right]

\displaystyle-(k_{1}(0)+1)k_{2}(j)\cdot\left[a^{\widehat{Q}_{j}}X_{Q,r}+b^{\widehat{Q}_{j}}\left(\frac{k_{2}(j)-1}{k_{2}(0)+k_{2}(j)}X_{Q,r}+\frac{k_{2}(0)+1}{k_{2}(0)+k_{2}(j)}X_{Q,s}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.+c^{\widehat{Q}_{j}}X_{Q,r}+\begin{cases}d^{\widehat{Q}_{j}}X_{Q,z_{2}}&\text{ if $2\in I_{0}$}\\
d^{\widehat{Q}_{j}}X_{Q,r}&\text{ if $2\in I_{j}$}\\
0&\text{ otherwise}\end{cases}\right]\right].

Moreover, we have:

(6.47)

\displaystyle WC

\displaystyle(Q^{+r})=-(-1)^{k_{2}(0)}\frac{\Gamma(\frac{1+r+\sum_{i=0}^{h}k_{1}(i)}{r})\Gamma(\frac{1+\sum_{i=0}^{h}k_{2}(i)}{s})}{\Gamma(\frac{1+r(I)}{r})\Gamma(\frac{1+s(I)}{s})}\cdot(k_{2}(0)+1)r\cdot

\displaystyle\left[a^{\widehat{Q}^{+r}}\left(\frac{k_{1}(0)+1}{k_{1}(0)+r}X_{Q,r}+\frac{r-1}{k_{1}(0)+r}X_{Q,s}\right)+b^{\widehat{Q}^{+r}}X_{Q,s}+c^{\widehat{Q}^{+r}}X_{Q,s}+\begin{cases}d^{\widehat{Q}^{+r}}X_{Q,z_{2}}&\text{ if $2\in I_{0}$}\\
0&\text{ otherwise}\end{cases}\right].

Lastly, we similarly have

(6.48)

\displaystyle WC

\displaystyle(Q^{+s})=(-1)^{k_{2}(0)}\frac{\Gamma(\frac{1+\sum_{i=0}^{h}k_{1}(i)}{r})\Gamma(\frac{1+s+\sum_{i=0}^{h}k_{2}(i)}{s})}{\Gamma(\frac{1+r(I)}{r})\Gamma(\frac{1+s(I)}{s})}\cdot(k_{1}(0)+1)s\cdot

\displaystyle\left[a^{\widehat{Q}^{+s}}X_{Q,r}+b^{\widehat{Q}^{+s}}\left(\frac{s-1}{k_{2}(0)+s}X_{Q,r}+\frac{k_{2}(0)+1}{k_{2}(0)+s}X_{Q,s}\right)+c^{\widehat{Q}^{+s}}X_{Q,r}+\begin{cases}d^{\widehat{Q}^{+s}}X_{Q,z_{2}}&\text{ if $2\in I_{0}$}\\
0&\text{ otherwise}\end{cases}\right].

We can then see that the coefficient of

X_{Q,r}
in (
6.39
) is proportional to,
20
20

20

After dividing by
(-1)^{k_{2}(0)}\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{1+\sum_{i=0}^{h}k_{1}(i)}{r}\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{1+\sum_{i=0}^{h}k_{2}(i)}{s}\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{1+r(I)}{r}\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{1+s(I)}{s}\right)}
. Note that the numerators for the
\Gamma
functions in the
WC(Q^{+r})
and
WC(Q^{+s})
cases are different and have an extra factor.

(6.49)

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{h}

\displaystyle\left(\left((k_{2}(0)+1)k_{1}(j)\frac{k_{1}(0)+1}{k_{1}(0)+k_{1}(j)}-(k_{1}(0)+1)k_{2}(j)\right)a^{\widehat{Q}_{j}}-(k_{1}(0)+1)k_{2}(j)\frac{k_{2}(j)-1}{k_{2}(0)+k_{2}(j)}b^{\widehat{Q}_{j}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.-(k_{1}(0)+1)k_{2}(j)c^{\widehat{Q}_{j}}-\mathbf{1}_{2\in I_{j}}(k_{1}(0)+1)k_{2}(j)d^{\widehat{Q}_{j}}\right)

\displaystyle-(k_{2}(0)+1)\frac{k_{1}(0)+1}{k_{1}(0)+r}(1+\sum_{i=0}^{h}k_{1}(i))a^{\widehat{Q}^{+r}}

\displaystyle+(k_{1}(0)+1)(1+\sum_{i=0}^{h}k_{2}(i))\left(a^{\widehat{Q}^{+s}}+\frac{s-1}{k_{2}(0)+s}b^{\widehat{Q}^{+s}}+c^{\widehat{Q}^{+s}}\right).

Similarly, the coefficient of
X_{Q,s}
is proportional to

(6.50)

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{h}

\displaystyle\left((k_{2}(0)+1)k_{1}(j)\frac{k_{1}(j)-1}{k_{1}(0)+k_{1}(j)}a^{\widehat{Q}_{j}}+\left((k_{2}(0)+1)k_{1}(j)-(k_{1}(0)+1)k_{2}(j)\frac{k_{2}(0)+1}{k_{2}(0)+k_{2}(j)}\right)b^{\widehat{Q}_{j}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+(k_{2}(0)+1)k_{1}(j)c^{\widehat{Q}_{j}}+\mathbf{1}_{2\in I_{j}}(k_{2}(0)+1)k_{1}(j)d^{\widehat{Q}_{j}}\right)

\displaystyle-(k_{2}(0)+1)(1+\sum_{i=0}^{h}k_{1}(i))\left(\frac{r-1}{k_{1}(0)+r}a^{\widehat{Q}^{+r}}+b^{\widehat{Q}^{+r}}+c^{\widehat{Q}^{+r}}\right)

\displaystyle+(k_{1}(0)+1)\frac{k_{2}(0)+1}{k_{2}(0)+s}(1+\sum_{i=0}^{h}k_{2}(i))b^{\widehat{Q}^{+s}}.

Note that the coefficient for
X_{Q,\times}
is always
0
. The coefficient of
X_{Q,z_{2}}
is
0
if
2\notin I_{0}
and otherwise it is proportional to

(6.51)

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{h}

\displaystyle\Big((k_{2}(0)+1)k_{1}(j)-(k_{1}(0)+1)k_{2}(j)\Big)d^{\widehat{Q}_{j}}

\displaystyle-(k_{2}(0)+1)(1+\sum_{i=0}^{h}k_{1}(i))d^{\widehat{Q}^{+r}}+(k_{1}(0)+1)(1+\sum_{i=0}^{h}k_{2}(i))d^{\widehat{Q}^{+s}}.

Consider a family
\{(a^{P},b^{P},c^{P},d^{P})\}_{P\in\mathcal{P}(I,\mathbf{d})}
that satisfies Property
Q1
(which we know exists by Lemma
6.17
). Then
d^{P}=0
and
a^{P}+b^{P}+c^{P}=1
for all
P
. Thus we have the constraint
c^{P}=1-a^{P}-b^{P}
for all
P
, so we may substitute this into (
6.49
) above and simplify. After factoring out
(k_{1}(0)+1)(k_{2}(0)+1)
, we can compute that the vanishing of (
6.49
) is equivalent to the equation

(6.52)

\frac{1+\sum_{i=0}^{h}k_{1}(i)}{k_{1}(0)+r}a^{\widehat{Q}^{+r}}+\frac{1+\sum_{i=0}^{h}k_{2}(i)}{k_{2}(0)+s}b^{\widehat{Q}^{+s}}=1+\sum_{j=1}^{h}\left(\frac{k_{1}(j)}{k_{1}(0)+k_{1}(j)}a^{\widehat{Q}_{j}}+\frac{k_{2}(j)}{k_{2}(0)+k_{2}(j)}b^{\widehat{Q}_{j}}\right).

But this is exactly Equation (
6.37
) so (
6.49
) vanishes. One can additionally check that after the same substitution, the vanishing of (
6.50
) is also equivalent to satisfying the equation (
6.52
). Lastly, note that the coefficient of
X_{Q,z_{2}}
immediately vanishes when
d^{P}=0
for all
P
. In summary, the coefficients of
X_{Q,\bullet}
vanish for all
Q\in\overline{\mathcal{Q}}(I,\mathbf{d})
if
\{(a^{P},b^{P},c^{P},d^{P})\}_{P\in\mathcal{P}(I,\mathbf{d})}
satisfies Property
Q1
, proving Item (1) of the Lemma.

Next, consider a family
\{(a^{P},b^{P},c^{P},d^{P})\}_{P\in\mathcal{P}(I,\mathbf{d})}
that satisfies Property
Q2
. Given
Q\in\overline{\mathcal{Q}}(I,\mathbf{d})
, note that we must have that
1\in I_{0}
and
2\in I_{0}\cup I_{1}
due to the convention of the ordering of the tuples. If
2\in I_{0}
, then
d^{\widehat{Q}_{j}}=1
and
a^{\widehat{Q}_{j}}=b^{\widehat{Q}_{j}}=c^{\widehat{Q}_{j}}=0
for all
j
. In turn, we have that
d^{\widehat{Q}^{+r}}=d^{\widehat{Q}^{+s}}=1
and
a^{\widehat{Q}^{+r}}=b^{\widehat{Q}^{+r}}=c^{\widehat{Q}^{+r}}=a^{\widehat{Q}^{+s}}=b^{\widehat{Q}^{+s}}=c^{\widehat{Q}^{+s}}=0
. Note that when
2\in I_{0}
, there are no summands with
d^{\widehat{Q}_{j}}
in Equations (
6.49
) or (
6.50
), so the corresponding coefficients of
X_{Q,r}
and
X_{Q,s}
vanish. Also, one can compute that the quantity in (
6.51
) vanishes when
d^{\widehat{Q}_{j}}=d^{\widehat{Q}^{+r}}=d^{\widehat{Q}^{+s}}=1
.

If
2\in I_{1}
, then the coefficient of
X_{Q,z_{2}}
vanishes, as stated above. In this case, we can see that
d^{\widehat{Q}_{1}}=1
and
a^{\widehat{Q}_{1}}=b^{\widehat{Q}_{1}}=c^{\widehat{Q}_{1}}=0
. On the other hand,
d^{\widehat{Q}_{j}}=0
for all
1<j\leq h
and
d^{\widehat{Q}^{+r}}=d^{\widehat{Q}^{+s}}=0
. We then can substitute using constraint that
c^{P}=1-a^{P}-b^{P}
for all
P\in\{\widehat{Q}_{2},\ldots,\widehat{Q}_{h},\widehat{Q}^{+r},\widehat{Q}^{+s}\}
to simplify (
6.49
) in terms of
a^{P}
and
b^{P}
. After doing so and factoring out
(k_{1}(0)+1)(k_{2}(0)+1)
, we can compute that the vanishing of (
6.49
) is equivalent to the equation

(6.53)

\frac{1+\sum_{i=0}^{h}k_{1}(i)}{k_{1}(0)+r}a^{\widehat{Q}^{+r}}+\frac{1+\sum_{i=0}^{h}k_{2}(i)}{k_{2}(0)+s}b^{\widehat{Q}^{+s}}=1+\sum_{j=2}^{h}\left(\frac{k_{1}(j)}{k_{1}(0)+k_{1}(j)}a^{\widehat{Q}_{j}}+\frac{k_{2}(j)}{k_{2}(0)+k_{2}(j)}b^{\widehat{Q}_{j}}\right).

This is exactly Equation (
6.38
), so (
6.49
) vanishes. One can additionally check that after the same substitution, that Equation (
6.50
) vanishes.
∎

We now turn our attention to
\Lambda\in\mathcal{G}_{\textup{Cont}}(P)
.

Notation 6.19
.

Let
\mathcal{P}_{Cont,h}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1})
be the collection of all tuples

\{(I_{0},k_{1}(0),k_{2}(0)),\ldots,(I_{h},k_{1}(h),k_{2}(h))\}

such that

•

1\in I_{0}

•

I_{j}
are disjoint,

•

\sqcup_{j=0}^{h}I_{j}=I
with at most one empty set, and

•

the graphs
\Gamma_{0,k_{1}(j),k_{2}(j),1,I_{j}}
are balanced with respect to
\mathbf{d}
for all
j
.

We write
\mathcal{P}_{Cont,h}^{\not=\emptyset}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1})

to be the set of those tuples in
\mathcal{P}_{Cont,h}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1})

with all
I_{j}\not=\emptyset
.

Consider a tuple
P^{\textup{Cont}}=\{(I_{0},k_{1}(0),k_{2}(0)),\ldots,(I_{h},k_{1}(h),k_{2}(h))\}\in\mathcal{P}_{Cont,h}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1})
. If for all
i\neq j
,
I_{i}
is nonempty, then we can define the element
\widehat{P}^{\textup{Cont}}_{j}\in\mathcal{P}_{h}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1})
given by

\widehat{P}_{j}^{\textup{Cont}}:=\{(I_{1},k_{1}(1),k_{2}(1)),\ldots,(I_{0}\cup I_{j},k_{1}(0)+k_{1}(j),k_{2}(0)+k_{2}(j))\ldots,(I_{h},k_{1}(h),k_{2}(h))\},

for any
1\leq j\leq h
.
In the case where
P^{\textup{Cont}}\in\mathcal{P}_{Cont,h}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1})
has the property that all
I_{j}
are nonempty, we may define,
similar to (
5.6
),
two new elements in
\mathcal{P}_{Cont,h+1}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1})

(6.54)

\displaystyle P^{Cont,+r}

\displaystyle=\{(I_{0},k_{1}(0),k_{2}(0)),\ldots,(I_{h},k_{1}(h),k_{2}(h)),(\emptyset,r,0)\};

\displaystyle P^{Cont,+s}

\displaystyle=\{(I_{0},k_{1}(0),k_{2}(0)),\ldots,(I_{h},k_{1}(h),k_{2}(h)),(\emptyset,0,s)\}.

Similar to (
6.36
), we define the
elements in
\mathcal{P}_{h+1}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1})

\widehat{P}^{Cont,+r}=(\widehat{P^{Cont,+r}})_{h+1};\qquad\widehat{P}^{Cont,+s}=(\widehat{P^{Cont,+s}})_{h+1}.

For
P^{\textup{Cont}}\in\mathcal{P}_{Cont,h}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1})
, there is a corresponding graph

(6.55)

\Gamma_{P^{\textup{Cont}}}:=\bigsqcup_{j=0}^{h}\Gamma_{0,k_{1}(j),k_{2}(j),1,\{(a_{i},b_{i})\}_{i\in I_{j}}}.

Let
\Gamma=\Gamma_{P}
with
P\in\mathcal{P}_{h}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1})
be a smooth graded balanced graph with an internal tail
1\in I(\Gamma)
. Suppose
\Lambda\in\mathcal{G}_{\textup{Cont}}(P)
as in Notation
6.7
. That is,
the graph
\Lambda
has connected components
\Xi_{1},\ldots,\Xi_{h}
and there is some
j
such that
\Xi_{j}
consists of a graph with two open vertices
v_{0}
and
v_{j}
with one edge between them so that:

•

The vertex
v_{0}
is rooted and has the internal tail
1
.

•

{\mathcal{B}}\Lambda
is balanced with respect to the descendent vector
\mathbf{d}
.

•

The half-edges
h_{0}
and
h_{j}
corresponding to the edge
e
satisfy the following:

\text{tw}(h_{0})=(0,0),{\text{alt}}(h_{0})=(0,0),\text{tw}(h_{j})=(r-2,s-2),\text{ and }{\text{alt}}(h_{j})=(1,1).

Set
I_{0}:=I(v_{0})
and
I_{j}:=I(v_{j})
. Note that
{\mathcal{B}}v_{0}=\Gamma_{0,k_{1}(v_{0}),k_{2}(v_{0}),1,\{(a_{i},b_{i})\}_{i\in I_{0}}}
and
{\mathcal{B}}v_{j}=\Gamma_{0,k_{1}(v_{j}),k_{2}(v_{j}),1,\{(a_{i},b_{i})\}_{i\in I_{j}}}
. Moreover, the graph
\Xi_{j}
is a degeneration of the smooth vertex graph
\Gamma_{0,k_{1}(v_{0})+k_{1}(v_{j}),k_{2}(v_{0})+k_{2}(v_{j}),1,\{(a_{i},b_{i})\}_{i\in I_{0}\cup I_{j}}}
, hence
{\mathcal{B}}\Lambda
corresponds to some tuple
P^{\textup{Cont}}\in\mathcal{P}_{Cont,h}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1})
and
P=\widehat{P}_{j}^{\textup{Cont}}
.

Note that there are precisely
(k_{1}(v_{0})+1)(k_{2}(v_{0})+1)
graphs in
\mathcal{G}_{\textup{Cont}}(P)
that have the base
{\mathcal{B}}\Lambda
and are a degeneration of
\Gamma
. Indeed, the graphs in
\mathcal{G}_{\textup{Cont}}(P)
with such properties are completely determined by two numbers:

•

R_{0}
the number of
r
-points in
v_{0}
that follow its root and precede the half-edge with respect to the cyclic order and

•

S_{0}
the number of
s
-points in
v_{0}
that follow its root and precede the half-edge with respect to the cyclic order.
21
21

21

We alert the reader that in this definition of

R_{0},S_{0}
, the role of
v_{0}
and
v_{j}
are the opposite of that used
for critical boundary graphs of
\mathcal{G}_{WC}(P)
. The reason for this is that we
prefer to keep the internal tail
1
attached to
v_{0}
for consistency.

We will denote the corresponding boundary graph by
\Gamma_{P,j,I_{0},k_{1}(0),k_{2}(0),R_{0},S_{0}}^{\textup{Cont}}
, where
0\leq R_{0}\leq k_{1}(v_{0})
and
0\leq S_{0}\leq k_{2}(v_{0})
.

The fiber
\Phi_{p}
of the map

(6.56)

\bigsqcup_{R_{0},S_{0}}F_{\Gamma_{P,j,I_{0},k_{1}(0),k_{2}(0),R_{0},S_{0}}^{\textup{Cont}}}:\bigsqcup_{R_{0},S_{0}}{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Gamma_{P,j,I_{0},k_{1}(0),k_{2}(0),R_{0},S_{0}}^{\textup{Cont}}}\to{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{{\mathcal{B}}\Lambda}

over a point
p\in{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{B}}\Lambda}
is the union of

k_{1}(v_{0})+k_{2}(v_{0})+1
closed intervals.
Indeed, the additional information of the location of the
forgotten non-twisted half-node requires a choice of a point between boundary markings.
The boundary
\partial\Phi_{p}
corresponds to the stable disks obtained by the forgotten half-node approaching a boundary marking. We pair endpoints corresponding to stable disks where one is obtained by the node approaching the marking from the right and the other one is obtained by letting the node approach the same marking from the left. Denote by
\hat{\Phi}_{p}
the quotient space of
\Phi_{p}
obtained by identifying the boundary points which are paired.
Thus,
\hat{\Phi}_{p}
is homeomorphic to
S^{1}
and is canonically identified with the boundary of the connected component indexed by
v_{0}

of the stable disk
\Sigma_{p}
corresponding to
p\in{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{{\mathcal{B}}\Lambda}
.

Observations 4.9 and 4.10 in
[
42
]
imply the following observation.

Observation 6.20
.

Note that when we forget the half-edge on
v_{0}
, the disk remains stable hence does not contract. By Observation
2.15
(ii), the restriction of the tautological line
{\mathbb{L}}_{i}|_{\hat{\Phi}_{p}}
is canonically trivial as
this line bundle is pulled back from a point of
{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{{\mathcal{B}}v_{0}}
.
With respect to this trivialization a canonical multisection of
{\mathbb{L}}_{i}|_{\Phi_{p}}
as well as
t_{r}^{v_{0}},t_{s}^{v_{0}},t_{\times}^{v_{0}}
, and
t_{z_{2}}^{v_{0}}
(if
z_{2}\in I_{0}
) restricted to
\Phi_{p}
are constant multivalued functions.
Indeed, in all these cases, these sections are pulled back from
{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{{\mathcal{B}}v_{0}}
.

On the other hand, Observation 4.11 and Lemma 4.12 in
[
42
]
imply that:

Observation 6.21
.

The section
t^{v_{0}}_{h_{0}}|_{\Phi_{p}}
descends to a continuous function
\theta_{p}:\hat{\Phi}_{p}\to\mathbb{C}^{\times}
. The winding number of
\theta_{p}
is
-1
.

The next observation, analogous to Observation
6.11
, follows again from direct inspection.

Observation 6.22
.

Let
P=\{(I_{i},k_{1}(i),k_{2}(i))\}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{h}(I,\mathbf{d}+{\bf e}_{1})
with

1\in I_{j}
for some
j
.
Let
\Lambda=\Gamma_{P,j,I_{0},k_{1}(0),k_{2}(0),R_{0},S_{0}}^{\textup{Cont}}\in\mathcal{G}_{\textup{Cont}}(P)

and
\Gamma=\Gamma_{P}
.
Consider the multisections of
{\mathbb{L}}_{1}\to{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}^{W}_{v_{0}}
given by
t_{r}
,
t_{s}
,
t_{\times}
and
t_{z_{2}}
from Notation
6.5
.
Then, by using the canonical identification of
Observation
2.15
,(i) of the pullback of
{\mathbb{L}}_{1}\to{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}^{W}_{v_{0}}
to
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}^{W}_{\Lambda}
with
({\mathbb{L}}_{1}\to{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}^{W}_{\Gamma})|_{{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}^{W}_{\Lambda}}
, we have

(1)

t_{r}|_{{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Lambda}}=k_{1}(v_{0})t^{v_{0}}_{r}\uplus k_{1}(v_{j})t^{v_{0}}_{h_{0}}
.

(2)

t_{s}|_{{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Lambda}}=k_{2}(v_{0})t^{v_{0}}_{s}\uplus k_{2}(v_{j})t^{v_{0}}_{h_{0}}
.

(3)

t_{\times}|_{{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Lambda}}=t^{v_{0}}_{{\times}}
.

(4)

If
2\in I_{j}
,

t_{z_{2}}|_{{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Lambda}}=\begin{cases}t^{v_{0}}_{z_{2}}&\text{if $2\in I_{0}$,}\\
t^{v_{0}}_{h_{0}}&\text{if $2\in I_{j}\setminus I_{0}$}.\end{cases}

We now compute the contributions of this case when we take the homotopy to a canonical multisection.

Lemma 6.23
.

Let
P\in\mathcal{P}_{h}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1})
, and
write
\Gamma:=\Gamma_{P}
as in (
5.3
). Let
t_{\bullet}
be one of the multisections of
{\mathbb{L}}_{1}\to{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Gamma}
defined in Notation
6.5
. Take the corresponding homotopy
H^{\Gamma}(t_{\bullet})
as defined in (
6.9
). Then

Z^{\Gamma_{P}}_{\textup{Cont}}(\mathbf{t}_{\bullet})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}P^{\textup{Cont}}\in\mathcal{P}_{Cont,h}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1}),\\
P^{\textup{Cont}}=\{(I_{i},k_{1}(i),k_{2}(i))\}_{i=0}^{h}\\
\text{where $\widehat{P}^{\textup{Cont}}_{j}=P$}\\
\text{ for some $j\in[h]$ and $1\in I_{0}$}\end{subarray}}\left(\prod_{i=0}^{h}\left\langle\prod_{j\in I_{i}}\tau_{d_{j}}^{(a_{j},b_{j})}\sigma_{1}^{k_{1}(i)}\sigma_{2}^{k_{2}(i)}\sigma_{12}\right\rangle^{\mathbf{s}^{\Gamma_{0,k_{1}(i),k_{2}(i),1,I_{i}}},o}\right)\beta_{P^{\textup{Cont}}}(\mathbf{t}_{\bullet}),

where

(6.57)

\beta_{P^{\textup{Cont}}}(\mathbf{t}_{\bullet}):=\begin{cases}\frac{k_{1}(j)}{k_{1}(0)+k_{1}(j)}&\text{if~}\bullet=r,\\
\frac{k_{2}(j)}{k_{2}(0)+k_{2}(j)}&\text{if~}\bullet=s,\\
0&\text{if }\bullet={\times},\\
0&\text{if }\bullet=z_{2},~\text{and }2\in I_{0},\\
1&\text{if }\bullet=z_{2},~\text{and }2\in I_{j}\setminus I_{0}.\end{cases}

Proof.

Fix
\Gamma=\Gamma_{P}
. First recall the definition of
Z^{\Gamma}_{\textup{Cont}}(\mathbf{t}_{w})
and the classification of graphs in
\mathcal{G}_{\textup{Cont}}(P)
. Note that, if
\Lambda=\Gamma_{P,j,I_{0},k_{1}(0),k_{2}(0),R_{0},S_{0}}^{\textup{Cont}}\in\mathcal{G}_{\textup{Cont}}(P)
, then
{\mathcal{B}}\Lambda=\Gamma_{P^{\textup{Cont}}}

as defined in (
6.55
)
where
P^{\textup{Cont}}=\{(I_{i},k_{1}(i),k_{2}(i))\}_{i=0}^{h}\in\mathcal{P}_{Cont,h}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1})
. Then we have that

(6.58)

\displaystyle Z^{\Gamma}_{\textup{Cont}}(\mathbf{t}_{w})

\displaystyle:=\sum_{\Lambda\in{\mathcal{G}}_{\textup{Cont}}(P)}\#Z(H|_{[0,1]\times{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Lambda}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}P^{\textup{Cont}}\in\mathcal{P}_{Cont,h}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1}),\\
P^{\textup{Cont}}=\{(I_{i},k_{1}(i),k_{2}(i))\}_{i=0}^{h}\\
\text{where $\widehat{P}^{\textup{Cont}}_{j}=P$}\\
\text{ for some $j\in[h]$}\end{subarray}}\sum_{R_{0},S_{0}}\#Z(H|_{[0,1]\times{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Gamma_{P,j,I_{0},k_{1}(0),k_{2}(0),R_{0},S_{0}}^{\textup{Cont}}}}).

Since
\mathbf{s}_{1}
is canonical, we have that

(6.59)

\#Z\left(\mathbf{s}_{1}^{{\mathcal{B}}\Lambda}\right)=\prod_{i=0}^{h}\langle\prod_{j\in I_{i}}\tau_{d_{j}}^{(a_{j},b_{j})}\sigma_{1}^{k_{1}(i)}\sigma_{2}^{k_{2}(i)}\sigma_{12}\rangle^{\mathbf{s}^{\Gamma_{0,k_{1}(i),k_{2}(i),1,I_{i}}},o}.

Write

(6.60)

\#Z\left(\mathbf{s}_{1}^{{\mathcal{B}}\Lambda}\right)=\sum_{p\in Z\left(\mathbf{s}_{1}^{{\mathcal{B}}\Lambda}\right)}\epsilon_{p},

where
\epsilon_{p}
is the weight of the zero
p
defined in (
2.18
). Recall that since
H
is of the form (
6.9
), the homotopy decomposes as
H=\pi^{*}\mathbf{s}_{1}\oplus H_{2}
, where

H_{2}(u):=\left((1-u)t_{\bullet}+u(1-u)\rho^{\Gamma}+u\mathbf{s}_{2}\right)\in C^{\infty}({\mathbb{L}}_{1}).

We can use this decomposition, along with the definition (
6.56
) of
\Phi_{p}
and the orientation of the boundary given in Theorem
2.58
(1),
to see that, for
\Lambda\in\mathcal{G}_{\textup{Cont}}(P)
:

(6.61)

\displaystyle\sum_{R_{0},S_{0}}\#Z\left(H|_{[0,1]\times{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Gamma_{P,j,I_{0},k_{1}(0),k_{2}(0),R_{0},S_{0}}^{\textup{Cont}}}}\right)=\sum_{R_{0},S_{0}}\#\left(Z\left(\pi^{*}F_{\Gamma_{P,j,I_{0},k_{1}(0),k_{2}(0),R_{0},S_{0}}^{\textup{Cont}}}^{*}\mathbf{s}_{1}^{{\mathcal{B}}\Lambda}\right)\cap Z(H_{2})\right)

\displaystyle\quad\quad=\#\left(\left(\bigcup_{R_{0},S_{0}}Z\left(\pi^{*}F_{\Gamma_{P,j,I_{0},k_{1}(0),k_{2}(0),R_{0},S_{0}}^{\textup{Cont}}}^{*}\mathbf{s}_{1}^{{\mathcal{B}}\Lambda}\right)\right)\cap Z(H_{2})\right)=\sum_{p\in Z\left(\mathbf{s}_{1}^{{\mathcal{B}}\Lambda}\right)}\epsilon_{p}\cdot\#Z\left(H_{2}|_{[0,1]\times\Phi_{p}}\right).

Note that the multisection
H_{2}|_{[0,1]\times\Phi_{p}}
descends to a multisection
\hat{H}_{2,p}
on the quotient space
{[0,1]\times\hat{\Phi}_{p}}
. Indeed, by Observation
6.20
, the multisections
\rho
,
\mathbf{s}_{2}
,
t_{r}^{v_{0}},t_{s}^{v_{0}},t_{\times}^{v_{0}}
, and
t_{z_{2}}^{v_{0}}
are constant for a fixed time and thus descend. Further, Observation
6.21
implies that
t_{h_{0}}^{v_{0}}
descends. We remark that as
H_{2}
is transverse on all boundary strata by Lemma
3.41
, we have that it does not vanish at the endpoints of the intervals in
\Phi_{p}
. By Observation
6.22
,
t_{\bullet}
is a multisum of the above multisections hence
H_{2}
descends as the components
t_{\bullet}
,

\rho
and
\mathbf{s}_{2}
descend. Thus,
\hat{H}_{2,p}
is a multisection of the trivial rank 1
\mathbb{C}
-bundle on

[0,1]\times\hat{\Phi}_{p}
and
\hat{H}_{2,p}
can be viewed as a

\mathbb{C}
-multivalued function on
[0,1]\times\hat{\Phi}_{p}
.
As a consequence,

(6.62)

\#Z\left(H_{2}|_{[0,1]\times\Phi_{p}}\right)=\#Z\left(\hat{H}_{2,p}\right).

By Observation
6.22
, we have the following
case by case description of
\hat{H}_{2,p}|_{\{0\}\times\hat{\Phi}_{p}}
:

(1)

if
\bullet=r
, then
\hat{H}_{2,p}|_{\{0\}\times\hat{\Phi}_{p}}=k_{1}(0)t_{r}\uplus k_{1}(j)\theta_{p}
;

(2)

if
\bullet=s
, then
\hat{H}_{2,p}|_{\{0\}\times\hat{\Phi}_{p}}=k_{2}(0)t_{s}\uplus k_{2}(j)\theta_{p}
;

(3)

if
\bullet={\times}
, then
\hat{H}_{2,p}|_{\{0\}\times\hat{\Phi}_{p}}=t_{\times}
;

(4)

if
\bullet=z_{2}
and
2\in I_{0}
, then
\hat{H}_{2,p}|_{\{0\}\times\hat{\Phi}_{p}}=t_{z_{2}}
; and

(5)

if
\bullet=z_{2}
and
2\in I_{j}\setminus I_{0}
, then
\hat{H}_{2,p}|_{\{0\}\times\hat{\Phi}_{p}}=\theta_{p}
.

Therefore, by Observations
6.20

and
6.21
, we have

(6.63)

\#Z\left(\hat{H}_{2,p}\right)=\beta_{P^{\textup{Cont}}}(\mathbf{t}_{\bullet}).

where
\beta_{P^{\textup{Cont}}}(\mathbf{t}_{\bullet})
is as in (
6.57
). Putting together equations (
6.59
), (
6.60
), (
6.61
), (
6.62
) and (
6.63
), we obtain the statement of the lemma.
∎

As in the case for the wall-crossing contributions given by the
Z_{WC}^{\Gamma_{P}}(\mathbf{t}_{\bullet})
, computing a closed form expression for
Z_{\textup{Cont}}^{\Gamma_{P}}(\mathbf{t}_{\bullet})
is hard, but the special linear combinations in Definition
6.15
will be meaningful.

Lemma 6.24
.

Let
\{(a^{P},b^{P},c^{P},d^{P})\}_{P\in\mathcal{P}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1})}
be a family of 4-tuples of rational numbers so that the 4-tuples do not depend on the order of the elements of
P
and
a^{P}+b^{P}+c^{P}+d^{P}=1
.

(1)

Suppose that
\{(a^{P},b^{P},c^{P},d^{P})\}_{P\in\mathcal{P}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1})}
satisfies Property
Q1
. Then for any
h\geq 0
and
P^{\textup{Cont}}=\{(I_{0},k_{1}(0),k_{2}(0)),\ldots,(I_{h},k_{1}(h),k_{2}(h))\}\in\mathcal{P}_{Cont,h}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1})
with
1\in I_{0}
, we have that

(6.64)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\frac{1+\sum_{i=0}^{h}k_{1}(i)}{r+k_{1}(0)}a^{\widehat{P}^{Cont,+r}}+&\frac{1+\sum_{i=0}^{h}k_{2}(i)}{s+k_{2}(0)}b^{\widehat{P}^{Cont,+s}}\\
&=1+\sum_{j=1}^{h}\left(\frac{k_{1}(j)}{k_{1}(0)+k_{1}(j)}a^{\widehat{P}^{\textup{Cont}}_{j}}+\frac{k_{2}(j)}{k_{2}(0)+k_{2}(j)}b^{\widehat{P}^{\textup{Cont}}_{j}}\right).\end{split}

(2)

Suppose that
\{(a^{P},b^{P},c^{P},d^{P})\}_{P\in\mathcal{P}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1})}
satisfies Property
Q2
. Then for any
h\geq 0
and
P^{\textup{Cont}}=\{(I_{0},k_{1}(0)+1,k_{2}(0)+1),\ldots,(I_{h},k_{1}(h),k_{2}(h))\}\in\mathcal{P}_{Cont,h}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1})
with
1\in I_{0}
and
2\notin I_{0}
, we have that

(6.65)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\frac{1+\sum_{i=0}^{h}k_{1}(i)}{r+k_{1}(0)}a^{\widehat{P}^{Cont,+r}}&+\frac{1+\sum_{i=0}^{h}k_{2}(i)}{s+k_{2}(0)}b^{\widehat{P}^{Cont,+s}}\\
&=1+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j=1\\
2\notin I_{j}\end{subarray}}^{h}\left(\frac{k_{1}(j)}{k_{1}(0)+k_{1}(j)}a^{\widehat{P}^{\textup{Cont}}_{j}}+\frac{k_{2}(j)}{k_{2}(0)+k_{2}(j)}b^{\widehat{P}^{\textup{Cont}}_{j}}\right).\end{split}

Proof.

Notice that

P^{\textup{Cont}}:=\{(I_{0},k_{1}(0),k_{2}(0)),\ldots,(I_{h},k_{1}(h),k_{2}(h))\}\in\mathcal{P}_{Cont,h}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1})

if and only if

Q:=\{(I_{0},k_{1}(0)+1,k_{2}(0)+1),\ldots,(I_{h},k_{1}(h),k_{2}(h))\}\in\mathcal{Q}_{h}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1}).

Moreover, we note that
\widehat{Q}_{j}=\widehat{P}^{\textup{Cont}}_{j}
and, if
Q\in\mathcal{Q}^{\neq 0}_{h}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1})
, then
\widehat{Q}^{+r}=\widehat{P}^{Cont,+r}
and
\widehat{Q}^{+s}=\widehat{P}^{Cont,+s}
. Note that

d^{\widehat{Q}_{j}}=0
if and only if
d^{\widehat{P}^{\textup{Cont}}_{j}}=0
.
It is then straightforward to see that (
6.37
) is equivalent to (
6.64
) and (
6.38
) is equivalent to (
6.65
).
∎

Lemma 6.25
.

Let
\{(a^{P},b^{P},c^{P},d^{P})\}_{P\in\mathcal{P}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1})}
be a family of 4-tuples of rational numbers so that the 4-tuples do not depend on the order of the elements of
P
and
a^{P}+b^{P}+c^{P}+d^{P}=1
.

(1)

Suppose that
\{(a^{P},b^{P},c^{P},d^{P})\}_{P\in\mathcal{P}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1})}
satisfies
Property
Q1
. Then

(6.66)

\displaystyle\sum_{h=1}^{|I|}\frac{1}{h!}

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}P\in\mathcal{P}_{h}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1}),\\
P=\{(I_{1},k_{1}(1),k_{2}(1)),\ldots,(I_{h},k_{1}(h),k_{2}(h))\}\end{subarray}}\frac{\Gamma(\frac{1+\sum_{i=1}^{h}k_{1}(i)}{r})\Gamma(\frac{1+\sum_{i=1}^{h}k_{2}(i)}{s})}{\Gamma(\frac{1+r(I)}{r})\Gamma(\frac{1+s(I)}{s})}\cdot

\displaystyle\quad\quad\cdot\left(a^{P}Z_{\textup{Cont}}^{\Gamma_{P}}(\mathbf{t}_{r})+b^{P}Z_{\textup{Cont}}^{\Gamma_{P}}(\mathbf{t}_{s})+c^{P}Z_{\textup{Cont}}^{\Gamma_{P}}(\mathbf{t}_{\times})+d^{P}Z_{\textup{Cont}}^{\Gamma_{P}}(\mathbf{t}_{z_{2}})\right)=-\mathcal{A}(I,\mathbf{d}).

(2)

Suppose that
\{(a^{P},b^{P},c^{P},d^{P})\}_{P\in\mathcal{P}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1})}
satisfies
Property
Q2
. Then

(6.67)

\displaystyle\sum_{h=1}^{|I|}\frac{1}{h!}

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}P\in\mathcal{P}_{h}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1}),\\
P=\{(I_{1},k_{1}(1),k_{2}(1)),\ldots,(I_{h},k_{1}(h),k_{2}(h))\}\end{subarray}}\frac{\Gamma(\frac{1+\sum_{i=1}^{h}k_{1}(i)}{r})\Gamma(\frac{1+\sum_{i=1}^{h}k_{2}(i)}{s})}{\Gamma(\frac{1+r(I)}{r})\Gamma(\frac{1+s(I)}{s})}\cdot

\displaystyle\quad\quad\cdot\left(a^{P}Z_{\textup{Cont}}^{\Gamma_{P}}(\mathbf{t}_{r})+b^{P}Z_{\textup{Cont}}^{\Gamma_{P}}(\mathbf{t}_{s})+c^{P}Z_{\textup{Cont}}^{\Gamma_{P}}(\mathbf{t}_{\times})+d^{P}Z_{\textup{Cont}}^{\Gamma_{P}}(\mathbf{t}_{z_{2}})\right)=0.

Proof.

For
h\geq 0
, let

\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{Cont,h}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1})
be the collection of elements

P^{\textup{Cont}}=\{(I_{0},k_{1}(0),k_{2}(0)),\ldots,(I_{h},k_{1}(h),k_{2}(h))\}

so that

•

I_{j}\neq\varnothing
for all
0\leq j\leq h
;

•

the elements
(I_{0},k_{1}(0),k_{2}(0)),\ldots,(I_{h},k_{1}(h),k_{2}(h))
are ordered in a way so that, for any two indices
i
and
j
, we have that
\text{min}(I_{i})<\text{min}(I_{j})
if and only if
i<j
;

•

the graph
\Gamma_{P^{\textup{Cont}}}
from (
6.55
) is balanced with respect to the descendent vector
\mathbf{d}
.

This implies that in particular
1\in I_{0}
and any
P^{\textup{Cont}}\in\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{Cont,h}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1})
with
h>0
belongs to
\mathcal{P}_{\textup{Cont}}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1})
.
In the case
h=0
, we just have the data of a graph balanced with respect to

\mathbf{d}
with internal markings
I
. Note that if
h\geq 1
, then
P^{\textup{Cont}}\in\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{Cont,h}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1})
is an element of
\mathcal{P}^{\neq\emptyset}_{Cont,h}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1})
.
Moreover, in this case, if we consider the action of the symmetric group

S_{h}
on
\mathcal{P}_{Cont,h}^{\neq\emptyset}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1})
given by permuting
the last
h
elements in the ordered partitions in

\mathcal{P}^{\neq\emptyset}_{Cont,h}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1})
, we can see that

\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{Cont,h}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1})
contains a unique element of each
orbit of this
S_{h}
-action.
Thus
\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{Cont,h}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1})
has precisely one element
for each graph
\Gamma_{P^{\textup{Cont}}}
, with the order of connected
components not taken into account.

We define
\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{Cont,h+1}^{r}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1})
and
\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{Cont,h+1}^{s}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1})
to be the collections of tuples that can be written in the form
P^{Cont,+r}
and
P^{Cont,+s}
, respectively, as
in (
6.54
) for some
P^{\textup{Cont}}\in\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{Cont,h}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1})
. Take

\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}_{Cont,h}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1}):=\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{Cont,h}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1})\cup\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{Cont,h}^{r}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1})\cup\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{Cont,h}^{s}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1}).

Define
\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}_{\textup{Cont}}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1}):=\bigcup_{h}\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}_{Cont,h}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1})
.

Set

O_{i}(P^{\textup{Cont}})=\left\langle\prod_{j\in I_{i}}\tau_{d_{j}}^{(a_{j},b_{j})}\sigma_{1}^{k_{1}(i)}\sigma_{2}^{k_{2}(i)}\sigma_{12}\right\rangle^{\mathbf{s}^{\Gamma_{0,k_{1}(i),k_{2}(i),1,I_{i}},o}}.

Using Lemma
6.23
and the fact that
P^{\textup{Cont}}
and
\widehat{P}^{\textup{Cont}}_{j}
have the same number of
r
-points and
s
-points, the left-hand side of both (
6.66
) and (
6.67
) can be expanded to:

(6.68)

\displaystyle\sum_{h=1}^{|I|}

\displaystyle\sum_{P\in\mathcal{P}_{h}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1})}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}P^{\textup{Cont}}\in\mathcal{P}_{Cont,h}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1}),\\
P^{\textup{Cont}}=\{(I_{i},k_{1}(i),k_{2}(i))\}_{i=0}^{h}\\
\text{where $\widehat{P}^{\textup{Cont}}_{j}=P$}\\
\text{ for some $j\in[h]$ and $1\in I_{0}$}\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{h!}\frac{\Gamma(\frac{1+\sum_{i=0}^{h}k_{1}(i)}{r})\Gamma(\frac{1+\sum_{i=0}^{h}k_{2}(i)}{s})}{\Gamma(\frac{1+r(I)}{r})\Gamma(\frac{1+s(I)}{s})}\cdot\left(\prod_{i=0}^{h}O_{i}(P^{\textup{Cont}})\right)\cdot

\displaystyle\left(\frac{k_{1}(j)}{k_{1}(0)+k_{1}(j)}a^{\widehat{P}^{\textup{Cont}}_{j}}+\frac{k_{2}(j)}{k_{2}(0)+k_{2}(j)}b^{\widehat{P}^{\textup{Cont}}_{j}}+\begin{cases}d^{\widehat{P}^{\textup{Cont}}_{j}}=1&\text{ if $2\in I_{j}$ and {\bf Q2} holds}\\
0&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}\right).

We can change the summations over
\mathcal{P}_{h}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1})
and special subsets of
\mathcal{P}_{Cont,h}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1})
to a summation over
\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}_{\textup{Cont}}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1})
. Given a
P^{\textup{Cont}}=\{(I_{i},k_{1}(i),k_{2}(i))\}_{i=0}^{h}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}_{\textup{Cont}}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1})
, we define the quantity:

Y_{P^{\textup{Cont}}}:=\frac{\Gamma(\frac{1+\sum_{i=0}^{h}k_{1}(i)}{r})\Gamma(\frac{1+\sum_{i=0}^{h}k_{2}(i)}{s})}{\Gamma(\frac{1+r(I)}{r})\Gamma(\frac{1+s(I)}{s})}\left(\prod_{i=0}^{h}O_{i}(P^{\textup{Cont}})\right).

Using the fact that there are
h!
elements of
\mathcal{P}_{Cont,h}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1})
in an
S_{h}
-orbit of a given element of
\mathcal{P}_{Cont,h}^{\not=\emptyset}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1})
, we can rewrite the summation given
in (
6.68
) as:

(6.69)

\sum_{P\in\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}_{\textup{Cont}}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1})}C(P),

where
C(P)
can be computed as follows from (
6.68
).

•

If
P=P^{\textup{Cont}}=\{(I_{i},k_{1}(i),k_{2}(i))\}_{i=0}^{h}\in\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{Cont,h}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1})
, then

C(P)=Y_{P^{\textup{Cont}}}\sum_{j=1}^{h}\left(\frac{k_{1}(j)}{k_{1}(0)+k_{1}(j)}a^{\widehat{P}^{\textup{Cont}}_{j}}+\frac{k_{2}(j)}{k_{2}(0)+k_{2}(j)}b^{\widehat{P}^{\textup{Cont}}_{j}}+\begin{cases}1&\text{ if $2\in I_{j}$ and {\bf Q2} holds}\\
0&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}\right).

•

If
P=P^{Cont,+r}=\{(I_{0},k_{1}(0),k_{2}(0)),\ldots,(I_{h},k_{1}(h),k_{2}(h)),(\emptyset,r,0)\}\in\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{Cont,h+1}^{r}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1})
, then using that
\langle\sigma_{1}^{r}\sigma_{12}\rangle=-1
and

\Gamma(x+1)=x\Gamma(x)
, we have that

\displaystyle C(P)

\displaystyle=\frac{\Gamma(\frac{1+r+\sum_{i=0}^{h}k_{1}(i)}{r})\Gamma(\frac{1+\sum_{i=0}^{h}k_{2}(i)}{s})}{\Gamma(\frac{1+r(I)}{r})\Gamma(\frac{1+s(I)}{s})}\left(\prod_{i=0}^{h}O_{i}(P^{\textup{Cont}})\right)\cdot\langle\sigma_{1}^{r}\sigma_{12}\rangle\cdot\left(\frac{r}{r+k_{1}(0)}a^{\widehat{P}^{Cont,+r}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1+\sum_{i=0}^{h}k_{1}(i)}{r}\cdot(-1)\cdot\frac{r}{r+k_{1}(0)}a^{\widehat{P}^{Cont,+r}}Y_{P^{\textup{Cont}}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1+\sum_{i=0}^{h}k_{1}(i)}{r+k_{1}(0)}a^{\widehat{P}^{Cont,+r}}Y_{P^{\textup{Cont}}}.

•

If
P=P^{Cont,+s}\in\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{Cont,h+1}^{s}(I,\mathbf{d})
, then we can analogously compute that

C(P)=-\frac{1+\sum_{i=0}^{h}k_{2}(i)}{s+k_{2}(0)}b^{\widehat{P}^{Cont,+s}}Y_{P^{\textup{Cont}}}.

View the quantities
Y_{P^{\textup{Cont}}}
for all
P^{\textup{Cont}}\in\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{Cont,h}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1})
as independent linear variables. Then we can write the left-hand side of both (
6.66
) and (
6.67
) as a linear combination of the variables
Y_{P^{\textup{Cont}}}
.

Consider a family of
4
-tuples satisfying Property
Q1
,
so that in particular
we fix
d^{P}=0
for all
P\in\mathcal{P}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1})
. Then the coefficient of
Y_{P^{\textup{Cont}}}
in
(
6.68
)
when Property
Q1
holds is, by Lemma
6.24
(1),

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{h}\Big(\frac{k_{1}(j)}{k_{1}(0)+k_{1}(j)}a^{\widehat{P}^{\textup{Cont}}_{j}}+

\displaystyle\frac{k_{2}(j)}{k_{2}(0)+k_{2}(j)}b^{\widehat{P}^{\textup{Cont}}_{j}}\Big)

\displaystyle-\frac{1+\sum_{i=0}^{h}k_{1}(i)}{r+k_{1}(0)}a^{\widehat{P}^{Cont,+r}}-\frac{1+\sum_{i=0}^{h}k_{2}(i)}{s+k_{2}(0)}b^{\widehat{P}^{Cont,+s}}=-1.

Thus we can simplify the left-hand of (
6.66
) to be

\displaystyle\sum_{P^{\textup{Cont}}\in\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{\textup{Cont}}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1})}-Y_{P^{\textup{Cont}}}

\displaystyle\qquad=\sum_{h\geq 0}\sum_{P\in\mathcal{P}_{h+1}(I,\mathbf{d})}\frac{-1}{(h+1)!}\frac{\Gamma(\frac{1+\sum_{i=0}^{h}k_{1}(i)}{r})\Gamma(\frac{1+\sum_{i=0}^{h}k_{2}(i)}{s})}{\Gamma(\frac{1+r(I)}{r})\Gamma(\frac{1+s(I)}{s})}\prod_{i=0}^{h}\langle\prod_{j\in I_{i}}\tau_{d_{j}}^{(a_{j},b_{j})}\sigma_{1}^{k_{1}(i)}\sigma_{2}^{k_{2}(i)}\sigma_{12}\rangle^{\mathbf{s}^{\Gamma_{0,k_{1}(i),k_{2}(i),1,I_{i}}},o}

\displaystyle\qquad=-\mathcal{A}(I,\mathbf{d}).

Note here we are replacing the sum over elements of

\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{Cont,h}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1})
with the set

\mathcal{P}_{h+1}(I,\mathbf{d})
and dividing by
(h+1)!
, as the set
of
S_{h+1}
-orbits of
\mathcal{P}_{h+1}(I,\mathbf{d})
is in one-to-one
correspondence with
\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{Cont,h}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1})
. Moreover, to fit with the convention of the indexing of
\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{Cont,h}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1})
going from
0
to
h
, we temporarily shift the indexing of tuples in
\mathcal{P}_{h+1}(I,\mathbf{d})
down by one.

Next consider a family of 4-tuples satisfying Property
Q2
. So, if
P
satisfies
\{1,2\}\subseteq I_{j}
for some
j
then
d^{P}=1
and
a^{P}=b^{P}=c^{P}=0
and otherwise
d^{P}=0
. Then the coefficient of
Y_{P^{\textup{Cont}}}
in the left-hand side of (
6.67
) is

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j=1\\
2\notin I_{j}\end{subarray}}^{h}

\displaystyle\left(\frac{k_{1}(j)}{k_{1}(0)+k_{1}(j)}a^{\widehat{P}^{\textup{Cont}}_{j}}+\frac{k_{2}(j)}{k_{2}(0)+k_{2}(j)}b^{\widehat{P}^{\textup{Cont}}_{j}}\right)+\sum_{j=1}^{h}\mathbf{1}_{2\in I_{j}}

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{1+\sum_{i=0}^{h}k_{1}(i)}{r+k_{1}(0)}a^{\widehat{P}^{Cont,+r}}-\frac{1+\sum_{i=0}^{h}k_{2}(i)}{s+k_{2}(0)}b^{\widehat{P}^{Cont,+s}}.

Note that if
2\in I_{0}
then
\{1,2\}\subseteq I_{0}
, and hence

d^{\widehat{P}^{\textup{Cont}}_{j}}=d^{\widehat{P}^{Cont,+r}}=d^{\widehat{P}^{Cont,+s}}=1

and the corresponding
a,b,c
terms are zero. Thus the above
expression is automatically zero.
Otherwise, we assume
2\not\in I_{0}
.
Since Property
Q2
holds, we have by Lemma
6.24
(2) that

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j=1\\
2\notin I_{j}\end{subarray}}^{h}

\displaystyle\left(\frac{k_{1}(j)}{k_{1}(0)+k_{1}(j)}a^{\widehat{P}^{\textup{Cont}}_{j}}+\frac{k_{2}(j)}{k_{2}(0)+k_{2}(j)}b^{\widehat{P}^{\textup{Cont}}_{j}}\right)+\sum_{j=1}^{h}\mathbf{1}_{2\in I_{j}}

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{1+\sum_{i=0}^{h}k_{1}(i)}{r+k_{1}(0)}a^{\widehat{P}^{Cont,+r}}-\frac{1+\sum_{i=0}^{h}k_{2}(i)}{s+k_{2}(0)}b^{\widehat{P}^{Cont,+r}}=-1+\sum_{j\neq 0}\mathbf{1}_{2\in I_{j}}=0.

Hence the coefficient of
Y_{P^{\textup{Cont}}}
vanishes for all
P^{\textup{Cont}}
and the expression in (
6.67
) vanishes.
∎

With all these computations in hand, we can take a deep breath, put this together, and prove Theorem
5.11
.

Proof of Theorem
5.11
.

Suppose
\{(a^{P},b^{P},c^{P},d^{P})\}_{P\in\mathcal{P}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1})}
is a family of
4
-tuples as constructed in Lemma
6.17
. Take a special canonical family
\mathbf{s}
of multisections bounded by
I
. By Theorem
5.4
, this choice will not affect the quantities of
\mathcal{A}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1})
or
\mathcal{A}(I,\mathbf{d})
.

We define the quantity

\#Z^{\Gamma_{P}}(\mathbf{t}_{a,b,c,d}):=a^{P}\int_{{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Gamma_{P}}}e(E,\mathbf{t}_{r})+b^{P}\int_{{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Gamma_{P}}}e(E,\mathbf{t}_{s})+c^{P}\int_{{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Gamma_{P}}}e(E,\mathbf{t}_{\times})+d^{P}\int_{{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Gamma_{P}}}e(E,\mathbf{t}_{z_{2}})

We then take the four homotopies
H^{\Gamma}(t_{\bullet})
from
\mathbf{t}_{\bullet}
to
\mathbf{s}
. We define analogously
\#Z^{\Gamma_{P}}(H^{\Gamma}_{a,b,c,d})
,
Z_{WC}^{\Gamma_{P}}(\mathbf{t}_{a,b,c,d})
,
Z_{\textup{Cont}}^{\Gamma_{P}}(\mathbf{t}_{a,b,c,d})
, and
Z_{\textup{Exchange}}^{\Gamma_{P}}(\mathbf{t}_{a,b,c,d})
as the appropriate weighted sums.

As usual, we write

(6.70)

\Gamma_{P,j}:=\Gamma_{0,k_{1}(j),k_{2}(j),1,\{(a_{i},b_{i})\}_{i\in I_{j}}};\qquad O_{j}(P):=\left\langle\prod_{i\in{I_{j}}}\tau^{(a_{i},b_{i})}_{d_{i}}\sigma_{1}^{k_{1}(j)}\sigma_{2}^{k_{2}(j)}\sigma_{12}\right\rangle^{\mathbf{s}^{\Gamma_{P,j}},o}

for any
P=\{(I_{j},k_{1}(j),k_{2}(j))\}\in\mathcal{P}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1})
.
We compute the quantity
\mathcal{A}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1})
given by

(6.71)

\sum_{h=1}^{|I|}\frac{1}{h!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}P\in\mathcal{P}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1})\\
P=\{(I_{i},k_{1}(i),k_{2}(i))\}_{i=1}^{h}\end{subarray}}\frac{\Gamma(\frac{1+\sum_{i=1}^{h}k_{1}(i)}{r})\Gamma(\frac{1+\sum_{i=1}^{h}k_{2}(i)}{s})}{\Gamma(\frac{1+r(I)}{r})\Gamma(\frac{1+s(I)}{s})}\left\langle\prod_{i\in{I(\Gamma_{P})}}\tau^{(a_{i},b_{i})}_{d_{i}+\delta_{1i}}\sigma_{1}^{k_{1}(\Gamma_{P})}\sigma_{2}^{k_{2}(\Gamma_{P})}\sigma_{12}^{k_{12}(\Gamma_{P})}\right\rangle^{\mathbf{s}^{\Gamma_{P}},o}

by applying Lemma
6.9
in the context of the quantity
\#Z^{\Gamma}(H_{a,b,c,d})
.
In this case, (
6.11
) becomes

(6.72)

\displaystyle\left\langle\prod_{i\in{I(\Gamma_{P})}}\tau^{(a_{i},b_{i})}_{d_{i}+\delta_{1i}}\sigma_{1}^{k_{1}(\Gamma_{P})}\sigma_{2}^{k_{2}(\Gamma_{P})}\sigma_{12}^{k_{12}(\Gamma_{P})}\right\rangle^{\mathbf{s}^{\Gamma_{P}},o}

\displaystyle=\#Z^{\Gamma_{P}}(\mathbf{t}_{a,b,c,d})+Z_{WC}^{\Gamma_{P}}(\mathbf{t}_{a,b,c,d})

\displaystyle+Z_{\textup{Cont}}^{\Gamma_{P}}(\mathbf{t}_{a,b,c,d})+Z_{\textup{Exchange}}^{\Gamma_{P}}(\mathbf{t}_{a,b,c,d}).

We now consider each term of the right hand side of (
6.72
) when considered as a linear combination given in (
6.71
).
Take a family of 4-tuples
\{(a^{P},b^{P},c^{P},d^{P})\}
that satisfies Property
Q1
.
In this case, since we know that
d^{P}=0
, the relevant multisections are
\mathbf{t}_{r}
,
\mathbf{t}_{s}
and
\mathbf{t}_{\times}
and these all point towards a boundary marking.
Let
P=\{(I_{j},k_{1}(j),k_{2}(j))\}\in\mathcal{P}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1})
with
1\in I_{1}
.

As in Lemma
6.4
, given the graph
\Gamma_{P,1}
and
A\subseteq I_{1}
, write
z_{A,a,b}
for an internal marking labelled by the union of labels of the marked points in
A
with twist
(r-2-a,s-2-b)
and write
v(P,1)_{A,a,b}
for the graph
\Gamma_{0,k_{1},k_{2},k_{12},\{(a_{i},b_{i})\}_{i\in(I_{1}\setminus A)\cup\{z_{A,a,b}\}}}
. Since
\mathbf{s}
is special canonical so is
\mathbf{s}_{1}
, hence by Lemma
6.4
and
Notation
6.3
we have

\displaystyle\#

\displaystyle Z^{\Gamma_{P,1}}(\mathbf{t}_{a,b,c,d})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}A\sqcup B=I_{1}\\
1\in A,\ |A|\geq 2\\
a\in\{-1,\ldots,r-2\},\\
b\in\{-1,\ldots,s-2\}\end{subarray}}\left\langle\tau_{0}^{(a,b)}\prod_{i\in A}\tau_{d_{i}}^{(a_{i},b_{i})}\right\rangle^{\text{ext}}\left\langle\tau_{0}^{(r-2-a,s-2-b)}\prod_{i\in B}\tau^{(a_{i},b_{i})}_{d_{i}}\sigma_{1}^{k_{1}(1)}\sigma_{2}^{k_{2}(1)}\sigma_{12}\right\rangle^{s^{v(P,1)_{A,a,b}},o}

Thus

(6.73)

\displaystyle\sum_{h=1}^{|I|}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{h!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}P\in\mathcal{P}_{h}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1})\\
P=\{(I_{i},k_{1}(i),k_{2}(i))\}_{i=1}^{h}\end{subarray}}\frac{\Gamma(\frac{1+\sum_{i=1}^{h}k_{1}(i)}{r})\Gamma(\frac{1+\sum_{i=1}^{h}k_{2}(i)}{s})}{\Gamma(\frac{1+r(I)}{r})\Gamma(\frac{1+s(I)}{s})}\cdot\#Z^{\Gamma_{P}}(\mathbf{t}_{a,b,c,d})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{h=1}^{|I|}\frac{1}{(h-1)!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}P\in\mathcal{P}_{h}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1})\\
P=\{(I_{i},k_{1}(i),k_{2}(i))\}_{i=1}^{h}\\
1\in I_{1}\end{subarray}}\frac{\Gamma(\frac{1+\sum_{i=1}^{h}k_{1}(i)}{r})\Gamma(\frac{1+\sum_{i=1}^{h}k_{2}(i)}{s})}{\Gamma(\frac{1+r(I)}{r})\Gamma(\frac{1+s(I)}{s})}\cdot\prod_{j=2}^{h}O_{j}(P)

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}A\sqcup B=I_{1}\\
1\in A,\ |A|\geq 2\\
a\in\{-1,\ldots,r-2\},\\
b\in\{-1,\ldots,s-2\}\end{subarray}}\left\langle\tau_{0}^{(a,b)}\prod_{i\in A}\tau_{d_{i}}^{(a_{i},b_{i})}\right\rangle^{\text{ext}}\left\langle\tau_{0}^{(r-2-a,s-2-b)}\prod_{i\in B}\tau^{(a_{i},b_{i})}_{d_{i}}\sigma_{1}^{k_{1}(1)}\sigma_{2}^{k_{2}(1)}\sigma_{12}\right\rangle^{s^{v(P,1)_{A,a,b}},o}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in\{-1,\ldots,r-2\}\\
b\in\{-1,\ldots,s-2\}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}A\sqcup B=I\\
1\in A,\ |A|\geq 2\end{subarray}}\left\langle\tau_{0}^{(a,b)}\prod_{i\in A}\tau_{d_{i}}^{(a_{i},b_{i})}\right\rangle^{\text{ext}}\cdot

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{h=1}^{|B|+1}\frac{1}{(h-1)!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}P\in\mathcal{P}_{h}(B\cup\{z_{a,b}\},\mathbf{d})\\
P=\{(I_{i},k_{1}(i),k_{2}(i))\}_{i=1}^{h}\\
z_{a,b}\in I_{1}\end{subarray}}\frac{\Gamma(\frac{1+\sum_{i=1}^{h}k_{1}(i)}{r})\Gamma(\frac{1+\sum_{i=1}^{h}k_{2}(i)}{s})}{\Gamma(\frac{1+r(I)}{r})\Gamma(\frac{1+s(I)}{s})}\prod_{j=2}^{h}O_{j}(P)\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left\langle\tau_{0}^{(r-2-a,s-2-b)}\prod_{i\in I_{1}\setminus\{z_{a,b}\}}\tau^{(a_{i},b_{i})}_{d_{i}}\sigma_{1}^{k_{1}(1)}\sigma_{2}^{k_{2}(1)}\sigma_{12}\right\rangle^{s^{\Gamma_{0,k_{1}(1),k_{2}(1),1,\{(a_{i},b_{i})\}_{i\in I_{1}\cup\{z_{a,b}\}}}},o}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in\{-1,\ldots,r-2\}\\
b\in\{-1,\ldots,s-2\}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}A\sqcup B=I\\
1\in A,\ |A|\geq 2\end{subarray}}\left\langle\tau_{0}^{(a,b)}\prod_{i\in A}\tau_{d_{i}}^{(a_{i},b_{i})}\right\rangle^{\text{ext}}\mathcal{A}(B\cup\{z_{a,b}\},\mathbf{d}).

By Lemma
6.18
, we know that if
\{(a^{P},b^{P},c^{P},d^{P})\}_{P\in\mathcal{P}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1})}
satisfies Property
Q1
, then

(6.74)

\sum_{h=1}^{|I|}\frac{1}{h!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}P\in\mathcal{P}_{h}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1})\\
P=\{(I_{i},k_{1}(i),k_{2}(i))\}_{i=1}^{h}\end{subarray}}\frac{\Gamma(\frac{1+\sum_{i=1}^{h}k_{1}(i)}{r})\Gamma(\frac{1+\sum_{i=1}^{h}k_{2}(i)}{s})}{\Gamma(\frac{1+r(I)}{r})\Gamma(\frac{1+s(I)}{s})}Z_{WC}^{\Gamma_{P}}(\mathbf{t}_{a,b,c,d})=0.

By Lemma
6.10
, we know that for all
P\in\mathcal{P}_{h}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1})
,

(6.75)

Z_{\textup{Exchange}}^{\Gamma_{P}}(\mathbf{t}_{a,b,c,d})=0.

Lastly, since
\{(a^{P},b^{P},c^{P},d^{P})\}_{P\in\mathcal{P}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1})}

satisfies Property
Q1
, we have that by Lemma
6.25
,

(6.76)

\sum_{h=1}^{|I|}\frac{1}{h!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}P\in\mathcal{P}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1})\\
P=\{(I_{i},k_{1}(i),k_{2}(i))\}_{i=0}^{h}\end{subarray}}\frac{\Gamma(\frac{1+\sum_{i=1}^{h}k_{1}(i)}{r})\Gamma(\frac{1+\sum_{i=1}^{h}k_{2}(i)}{s})}{\Gamma(\frac{1+r(I)}{r})\Gamma(\frac{1+s(I)}{s})}Z_{\textup{Cont}}^{\Gamma_{P}}(\mathbf{t}_{a,b,c,d})=-\mathcal{A}(I,\mathbf{d}).

By substituting into (
6.71
) the relation given in (
6.72
) and then simplifying using (
6.73
), (
6.74
), (
6.75
), and (
6.76
),
we obtain the open topological recursion relation (
5.38
).

We next turn to the case where
|I|\geq 2
.
Take a family of 4-tuples
\{(a^{P},b^{P},c^{P},d^{P})\}
that satisfies Property
Q2
. In this case, the computation for
\#Z^{\Gamma_{P,1}}(\mathbf{t}_{a,b,c,d})
for
\Gamma=\Gamma_{P}
has two cases, depending on whether or not the markings
1
and
2
are in the same connected component.
Let
P=\{(I_{j},k_{1}(j),k_{2}(j))\}\in\mathcal{P}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1})

with
1\in I_{1}
.
If
2\notin I_{1}
then
d^{P}=0
and the relevant multisections
\mathbf{t}_{\bullet}
point to only boundary markings. We then have that

\displaystyle\#

\displaystyle Z^{\Gamma_{P,1}}(\mathbf{t}_{a,b,c,d})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}A\sqcup B=I_{1}\\
1\in A,|A|\geq 2\\
a\in\{-1,\ldots,r-2\},\\
b\in\{-1,\ldots,s-2\}\end{subarray}}\left\langle\tau_{0}^{(a,b)}\prod_{i\in A}\tau_{d_{i}}^{(a_{i},b_{i})}\right\rangle^{\text{ext}}\left\langle\tau_{0}^{(r-2-a,s-2-b)}\prod_{i\in B}\tau^{(a_{i},b_{i})}_{d_{i}}\sigma_{1}^{k_{1}(1)}\sigma_{2}^{k_{2}(1)}\sigma_{12}\right\rangle^{s^{v(P,1)_{A,a,b}},o}

Otherwise, if
2\in I_{1}
then
d^{P}=1
and
a^{P}=b^{P}=c^{P}=0
so the relevant multisection is
\mathbf{t}_{z_{2}}
which points towards the internal marking
z_{2}
, hence we have that

\displaystyle\#

\displaystyle Z^{\Gamma_{P,1}}(\mathbf{t}_{a,b,c,d})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}A\sqcup B=I_{1}\\
1\in A,2\in B\\
|A|\geq 2\\
a\in\{-1,\ldots,r-2\},\\
b\in\{-1,\ldots,s-2\}\end{subarray}}\left\langle\tau_{0}^{(a,b)}\prod_{i\in A}\tau_{d_{i}}^{(a_{i},b_{i})}\right\rangle^{\text{ext}}\left\langle\tau_{0}^{(r-2-a,s-2-b)}\prod_{i\in B}\tau^{(a_{i},b_{i})}_{d_{i}}\sigma_{1}^{k_{1}(1)}\sigma_{2}^{k_{2}(1)}\sigma_{12}\right\rangle^{s^{v(P,1)_{A,a,b}},o}.

Note in both cases
2
is never an internal marking of a closed vertex
and we will get a contribution whenever
2
is an internal marking of an
open vertex.
Through a similar resummation as before in (
6.73
) we get that

(6.77)

\displaystyle\sum_{h=1}^{|I|}\frac{1}{h!}

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}P\in\mathcal{P}_{h}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1})\\
P=\{(I_{i},k_{1}(i),k_{2}(i))\}_{i=1}^{h}\\
1\in I_{1}\end{subarray}}\frac{\Gamma(\frac{1+\sum_{i=1}^{h}k_{1}(i)}{r})\Gamma(\frac{1+\sum_{i=1}^{h}k_{2}(i)}{s})}{\Gamma(\frac{1+r(I)}{r})\Gamma(\frac{1+s(I)}{s})}\cdot\#Z^{\Gamma_{P}}(\mathbf{t}_{a,b,c,d})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in\{-1,\ldots,r-2\}\\
b\in\{-1,\ldots,s-2\}\end{subarray}}\ \sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}A\coprod B=I\\
1\in A,\ 2\in B\\
|A|\geq 2\end{subarray}}\left\langle\tau_{0}^{(a,b)}\prod_{i\in A}\tau_{d_{i}}^{(a_{i},b_{i})}\right\rangle^{\text{ext}}\mathcal{A}(B\cup\{z_{a,b}\},\mathbf{d}).

Lastly, recall that when
\{(a^{P},b^{P},c^{P},d^{P})\}_{P\in\mathcal{P}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1})}
satisfies Property
Q2
, we have by Lemma
6.18

that (
6.74
) still holds. Further,
Lemma
6.25
implies that

(6.78)

\sum_{h=1}^{|I|}\frac{1}{h!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}P\in\mathcal{P}_{h}(I,\mathbf{d}+{{\mathbf{e}}}_{1})\\
P=\{(I_{i},k_{1}(i),k_{2}(i))\}_{i=1}^{h}\end{subarray}}\frac{\Gamma(\frac{1+\sum_{i=1}^{h}k_{1}(i)}{r})\Gamma(\frac{1+\sum_{i=1}^{h}k_{2}(i)}{s})}{\Gamma(\frac{1+r(I)}{r})\Gamma(\frac{1+s(I)}{s})}Z_{\textup{Cont}}^{\Gamma_{P}}(\mathbf{t}_{a,b,c,d})=0.

Concluding this computation, by substituting into (
6.71
) the relation given in (
6.72
) and then simplifying using (
6.77
), (
6.74
), (
6.75
), and (
6.78
), we obtain the open topological recursion relation
(
5.39
).
∎
